- Prevention: any job that combines `ref: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }}` with `run:` (or `uses: ./...`) commands executing checked-out code MUST ALSO carry an `if:` guard restricting to operator-owned branches: `if: github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository`. Fork PRs SKIP the job entirely (the gate is then enforced only on branches the operator owns). The alternative — switching to `pull_request_target` + a pinned trusted ref — is more correct in principle but pulls in head-sha label/body coupling complexity; for single-author repos the `if:` guard is the smallest-surface fix. Lint rule candidate: a pytest scan of `.github/workflows/*.yml` that asserts every job using `head.sha` checkout also carries the same-repo predicate.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` `perf-claim-lint` job (`if:` predicate with `head.repo.full_name == github.repository`); `tests/scripts/test_perf_bench_lint.py::test_20_workflow_perf_claim_lint_has_same_repo_guard` (asserts the predicate is present; a future job that needs head-sha checkout MUST extend this test or land its own equivalent).

### strip-then-split inconsistency in markdown-section detection

- First seen in: 2026-05-26 — PR #1357 (issue #1356) bot review iter-2 WARNING. `scripts/perf_bench/lint_pr_artifacts.py:_slice_bypass_section` used `_has_header_line(body, HEADER_BYPASS)` (operates on HTML-comment-stripped body) as the guard, then sliced via `body.split(HEADER_BYPASS, 1)` on the RAW body. A header line with a trailing/embedded HTML comment (`## Bypass justification<!-- x -->`) makes the guard fire (line strips to the header text) while the raw split can land at a different occurrence — or leak HTML-comment debris into the downstream regex search window.
- Symptom: a function uses one canonicalisation pass to GATE a downstream `str.split(needle, ...)` / `str.find(needle)` / regex `.search(needle)` operation that runs on the ORIGINAL string. Whenever the canonicalisation changes the string (HTML comments stripped, whitespace normalised, Unicode folded, …) the guard's view and the slice's view drift. The drift surface is silent: code looks correct because both expressions name the same `needle`, but they operate on different `haystack`s.
- Prevention: when a function uses `_has_header_line` (or any other guard that operates on a canonicalised view of the body) to gate a subsequent `str.split` / `str.find` / regex search, the slice MUST use the same canonicalised view, not the raw body. Pattern: `cleaned = _strip_html_comments(body); if not _has_header_line(cleaned, h): return None; after = cleaned.split(h, 1)[1]`. Mechanical check during pre-flight review: grep for `_has_header_line` callers and confirm each follow-up `split` / `find` / regex search names the same variable that was passed to the helper.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `scripts/perf_bench/lint_pr_artifacts.py:_slice_bypass_section` (uses `cleaned` for both guard + split); `tests/scripts/test_perf_bench_lint.py::test_21_bypass_html_comment_trailing_header_does_not_misslice` (regression: bypass header with trailing HTML comment still slices the section + finds the Operator/Reason lines).

### Unquoted SQL identifier in shell-out harness

- First seen in: 2026-05-26 — PR #1357 (issue #1356) bot review iter-3 WARNING. `scripts/perf_bench/_run_explain.py:_row_count` shelled out via `psql -c "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}"` where `table` came from an operator-authored per-ticket YAML. The `--check-floors-only` path validated `table` against `floors.yaml`, but the `main()` path called `_row_count` directly after `cfg["target_table"]` without re-checking. A YAML value like `x; DROP TABLE financial_facts_raw` would execute arbitrary SQL against the bench DB. The operator authors the YAML so escalation is bounded, but the exposed surface (`psql -c` shell-out + unquoted identifier) is the same bug class as any other unquoted-identifier SQL injection — fix the shape, not the threat model.
- Symptom: a script (Python helper, shell wrapper, etc.) builds a SQL string by f-string-interpolating a caller-supplied identifier (`table`, `column`, `schema`, `view`) into an `EXPLAIN` / `SELECT` / `COUNT(*)` and pipes it to `psql -c`. The interpolation is unquoted because `psycopg.sql.Identifier` doesn't apply to a `subprocess` invocation. Whoever can write the source of that identifier — config file, env var, CI variable, even an operator who has direct shell access — owns the SQL surface. The trust boundary is invisible because the value looks innocuous at the call site.
- Prevention: any helper that shells out to `psql` (or any other DB CLI) with an interpolated identifier MUST validate the value against a strict identifier regex (`^[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*$` for our codebase; tighten or widen per dialect) at the chokepoint, not at the loader. The chokepoint guard is non-negotiable so a future caller cannot bypass loader-side validation. Pattern: `if not _TABLE_IDENT_RE.fullmatch(table): _err(...); out = _psql(db_url, f"... FROM {table}")`. Secondary defence: validate at the loader too so the error surfaces early. If `psycopg` is in-process, prefer `psycopg.sql.SQL("...{}").format(sql.Identifier(table))` over any shell-out at all.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `scripts/perf_bench/_run_explain.py:_row_count` + `_load_ticket_config` (both guards on `target_table`); `scripts/perf_bench/_run_explain.py:TABLE_IDENT_RE` (the shared regex constant); `tests/scripts/test_perf_bench_lint.py::test_22_harness_table_ident_regex_rejects_sql_fragments` (positive + negative cases incl. `x; DROP TABLE ...`, quote-escape, uppercase, leading digit, dash, newline, empty).

### CHECK constraints land in ALTER TABLE migrations, not just CREATE TABLE

- First seen in: 2026-05-27 — Phase 0 NEW-B + NEW-C bundle (PR phase-0-new-b-c-bundle) Codex 2 pre-push review. `scripts/perf_bench/seed_synthetic_fixture/seed_ownership_institutions_current.py:80` emitted `filer_cik = f"SYN{f:08d}"` (e.g. `"SYN00000000"`) based on a grep of `sql/114_ownership_institutions_observations.sql` that confirmed only `filer_cik TEXT NOT NULL` with no CHECK on the CREATE TABLE. The follow-on `sql/134_ownership_identifier_check_constraints.sql:54-58` ALTER TABLE added `CHECK (filer_cik ~ '^[0-9]{10}$')` — applied to the live dev DB at apply time. The seeder would have aborted on the first COPY row against any migrated bench DB.
- Symptom: a writer (seeder, fixture, parser, ingester) is built from a column inventory that greps only the CREATE TABLE statement. Subsequent ALTER TABLE migrations (`ADD CONSTRAINT`, `ADD COLUMN`, `ALTER COLUMN`, DROP, ALTER DEFAULT) have already changed the live schema but stay invisible to the writer's author. The writer compiles, ruff/pyright pass, unit tests pass (no DB), then fails on the first real INSERT/COPY at runtime — or worse, silently passes on a partial schema (dev DB applied without the later migration).
- Prevention: when grepping for column constraints, ALWAYS run BOTH:
  ```bash
  rg -n "CREATE TABLE.*<table>" sql/
  rg -n "ALTER TABLE.*<table>" sql/
  rg -n "ADD CONSTRAINT.*<column>" sql/  # catches column-specific renames
  ```
  Treat the union as authoritative, not the CREATE TABLE alone. When dispatching an investigation sub-agent, tell it explicitly "list every CHECK / NOT NULL / FK across CREATE TABLE AND every ALTER TABLE migration that touches this column". Add a same-class-scan row in the SQL skill so the pattern hardens.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/sql-correctness.md` "Constraints live in two places" section (the bash one-liner above); `scripts/perf_bench/seed_synthetic_fixture/seed_ownership_institutions_current.py:_synthetic_filer_cik` (helper that produces 10-digit numeric CIK with overflow guard); `tests/scripts/test_seed_synthetic_fixture.py::test_generate_rows_check_constraints` (regression: every emitted row's `filer_cik` must satisfy the sql/134 regex) + `::test_synthetic_filer_cik_overflow_guard` (overflow refusal) + `::test_synthetic_filer_cik_rejects_negative`.

### Agent narrates "waiting" instead of polling

- First seen in: 2026-05-27 / 2026-05-28 — Phase 0 close R1 measurement session. Agent pushed PR #1364 (issue #1363) then narrated "Waiting on bot review + CI. Will resume #1363 cycle when verdict returns" and stopped the turn. Later pushed PR #1366 (issue #1365) then narrated "Awaiting bot review verdicts on both. Will drive iterations on whichever comes back first" and stopped again. Operator had to manually nudge the agent twice. Both stops happened despite memory entries `[[feedback_post_push_cycle]]` ("IMMEDIATELY after every push/PR-create, start polling. No 'let me know if'.") and `[[feedback_no_fake_polling]]` ("Never narrate 'waiting/polling/monitoring' without invoking actual check tool that turn"). The memory rules existed; the agent ignored them in practice.
- Symptom: any turn that ends with an open-ended "awaiting" / "standing by" / "will resume when X returns" / "tell me when..." / "let me know if..." phrase and no follow-on tool call. The trigger is usually a push (push → CI cadence → tempting to stop), but it can also fire after kicking off a long Codex review, a long pytest run, a long-running operator action (bootstrap run, deploy, queue drain), or any non-instant external state change.
- Prevention: whenever the agent is "waiting" on ANY external state change it MUST stay actively engaged. Required substitutes for sit-and-wait:
  1. **Push / PR-create** → start a Monitor (per-event poll) OR run `gh pr checks <n>` + `gh pr view <n> --comments` in successive Bash invocations between which useful parallel work happens. Continue until the bot review verdict + every CI check has terminal status on the latest commit.
  2. **Long-running Codex review** → `codex exec review` is synchronous; the agent's turn does NOT end while it runs. Wait for the call to return, then parse + act in the same turn.
  3. **Operator-driven measurement run** (e.g. R1 bootstrap) → poll DB state (bootstrap_runs / bootstrap_stages / job_runtime_heartbeat / dispatcher idle JSONL size) via Bash or Monitor. Surface anomalies as they appear. Never stop with "tell me when R1 finishes" — set a Monitor that emits on terminal state.
  4. **External CI on remote** → Monitor with a poll loop that emits per check completion + exits when all terminal. Do NOT ScheduleWakeup mid-PR-cycle (banned per `[[feedback_no_sleepy_claude]]`).
- Allowed substitutes when there is genuinely no parallel work AND the wait is bounded: a Monitor armed for the specific terminal-state condition, OR a `Bash run_in_background` `until <cond>; do <check>; sleep N; done` that exits when the condition is satisfied. The agent must arm one of these instead of stopping the turn.
- Banned phrasing (regression sentinel): "Standing by", "Awaiting verdict", "Will resume when X", "Let me know when...", "Tell me when..." (when the agent could poll instead).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; tracked under issue #1367. **Active Stop-hook enforcement landed (#1369): `.claude/hooks/stop_narrate_waiting.py`** (wired via the `Stop` hook in `.claude/settings.json`). It blocks a turn whose FINAL assistant message contains a banned token, injecting a poll-instead reminder. Matches only the last message (no whole-transcript false positives) and honours `stop_hook_active` (one nudge per stop-cycle, no infinite block). Spec: `docs/specs/tooling/2026-06-28-stop-narrate-waiting-hook.md`. Scope note: Stop only — UserPromptSubmit deferred (the unattended loop ends turns on Stop, not on user prompts). Memory cross-link: `[[feedback_post_push_cycle]]` + `[[feedback_no_fake_polling]]` reinforced with the explicit "any wait, not just post-push" extension.

### Dev-sized EXPLAIN ≠ prod-sized EXPLAIN — never accept a partition-heavy MERGE perf claim from an empty/dev DB

- First seen in: 2026-05-30 — #1345 PR-A (S22 MERGE-cliff batch refresh). The whole headline win (batch the per-instrument `refresh_*_current` MERGE so the ~124-partition index probe on `ownership_*_observations` is amortised over N instruments) cannot be validated on dev because dev PG was fresh-empty this cycle and an `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` over empty/near-empty partitions reports planning + execution costs that bear no relation to the prod-sized shape that produced the original `shared hit=18825` / 1043 ms-per-MERGE receipt.
- Symptom: a perf-motivated change ships with an acceptance gate phrased as "median 3-trial <200 ms" or "EXPLAIN shows no Seq Scan" — and that gate is run against a dev/test DB with a handful of rows. The plan reads as "perf verified" but the measured numbers are meaningless: partition-probe cost scales with the number of *populated* partitions and per-partition row counts, neither of which exists on an empty DB. Worse, an empty DB can make a genuinely-slow plan look instant, hiding a regression.
- Prevention: for any change whose value is a query-performance improvement on a partitioned / large table, the **merge gate is functional correctness** (equivalence / idempotence / scope-clamp contract tests on the small test DB), and the **perf gate is explicitly deferred to a post-bootstrap measurement on prod-sized data** — recorded in the PR as operator-owned, never asserted at merge from a dev EXPLAIN. State the deferral in the spec's acceptance section so a reviewer doesn't read "tests pass" as "perf proven". The headline perf claim must additionally be guarded by a *wiring* regression test (proving the fast path is actually called) since the perf number itself is unverifiable at merge.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `docs/proposals/etl/1345-s22-merge-cliff.md` §5 (acceptance gate split: functional = merge gate, perf = operator post-bootstrap) + §6 (wiring-regression test requirement); `tests/test_ownership_observations_refresh_batch.py` (equivalence/idempotence/scope-clamp contracts are the merge gate, not any timing assertion).
- Verification step: grep a perf-motivated PR's acceptance section for a hard latency/EXPLAIN number. If present, confirm it is tagged "measured post-bootstrap on prod-sized data" with the run recorded — otherwise it is a dev-sized EXPLAIN and must be downgraded to a deferred operator gate. Confirm a wiring/contract test guards the fast path independently of the (unverifiable-at-merge) timing.

### Dual-location gate added to pre-push hook but not mirrored into CI

- First seen in: #1382 / #1387 (chokepoint-lint drift); pattern extracted in #1329 (PR #1391).
- Symptom: a lint guard lived only in `.githooks/pre-push`, so a `--no-verify` push of a regression it was meant to catch landed green at CI. The pre-push hook and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` are two copies of the same gate set; adding a guard to one and forgetting the other silently re-opens the bypass.
- Prevention: any gate that must run in two locations needs a parity guard that fails when the two drift, not just a one-time sync. For the pre-push↔CI lint set this is `scripts/check_ci_mirrors_prepush.sh` (wired into BOTH the hook and CI): it diffs the `bash scripts/check_*.sh` invocations in each file and exits non-zero on mismatch. When adding a new dual-location gate of any kind, add (or extend) a parity guard alongside it — do not rely on remembering to update both copies.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `scripts/check_ci_mirrors_prepush.sh`; wired into `.githooks/pre-push` + `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `tests/test_ci_mirrors_prepush.py` (live parity + drift-detection).
- Verification step: `bash scripts/check_ci_mirrors_prepush.sh` must exit 0; the `bash scripts/check_*.sh` set in `.githooks/pre-push` must equal the set in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the guard excludes itself).

### Leaked pytest worker/mig DBs (datconnlimit=-2 corpses) bloated dev-PG to 13.1M files

- First seen in: 2026-05-30 — dev-PG data dir hit 13,107,680 files (#1401). Root cause: `kill -9`'d xdist workers (the recurring pre-push xdist + PG-lock OOM event) and SIGKILL'd migration-replay tests skip `finally` / `pytest_sessionfinish` teardown, leaking their private DBs (`ebull_test_*_gwN`, `ebull_mig*`). Some leaked as `datconnlimit = -2` corpses (PG marks a DB `-2` when a `DROP DATABASE` is interrupted mid-flight — it then refuses all connections and can only be dropped). Every `docker restart ebull-postgres` over the bloated dir then paid a ~30-min chown+fsync tax (both file-count-bound).
- Symptom: the controller-start orphan sweep (`_drop_orphan_workers_older_than`) left the leak in place because it (a) matched only `ebull_test%` — never `ebull_mig*`; (b) gated on a 1h parsed-epoch age; (c) skipped when `CI=true`; (d) used plain `DROP` without `WITH (FORCE)`, which cannot evict a `-2` corpse (connection-refused → wedged backend blocks the drop). So the worst leaks — exactly the ones a `kill -9` produces — were the ones the sweep structurally could not clear. Separately, the per-worker DB is reused across the whole session with `TRUNCATE`-only per-test cleanup, so any test that `CREATE`s relations without dropping them accumulates them silently; one runaway pushed a worker DB past ~2.1M relations with no failing test to flag it.
- Prevention: two new rails in `tests/fixtures/ebull_test_db.py`, both wired at controller-start / fixture-teardown. (1) `_force_drop_invalid_test_dbs` force-drops every `ebull_test_*`/`ebull_mig*` DB with `datconnlimit = -2` — unconditionally safe (a `-2` DB is connection-refused, so no concurrent-invocation race; no age gate needed), `_NEVER_DROP` skip protects the template. (2) `_assert_worker_relations_under_ceiling` runs in `ebull_test_conn` teardown and fails the first test that pushes `count(*) FROM pg_class` past `_WORKER_DB_RELATION_CEILING` (50k; template ≈9.6k) — a relation leak now surfaces as a named failing test, not a silent disk disaster. The operator escape hatch `tests/fixtures/cleanup_test_dbs.py` was widened to both families + documented force-drop semantics. When teardown must survive `kill -9`, a bare `try/finally` is insufficient — the auto-reaper at next session start is the kill-9-proof backstop.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/fixtures/ebull_test_db.py` (`_force_drop_invalid_test_dbs`, `_assert_worker_relations_under_ceiling`, `_WORKER_DB_RELATION_CEILING`); `tests/fixtures/cleanup_test_dbs.py`; `tests/test_orphan_sweep.py` (reaper + tripwire regression tests); `.claude/skills/engineering/test-quality.md` (dev-DB isolation invariant rails 4 + 6).
- Verification step: `SELECT datname, datconnlimit FROM pg_database WHERE datname LIKE 'ebull_test%' OR datname LIKE 'ebull_mig%'` should show no `-2` rows after a controller-start. Never `docker restart ebull-postgres` on a bloated data dir (chown+fsync tax ~30 min); clear the leak (cleanup tool) or kill the specific wedged backend in-container first.

### Dropping a bootstrap stage orphans its job_name from the source registry

- First seen in: #1413 (PR #1414) — bulk-only bootstrap dropped 8 per-CIK stages from `_BOOTSTRAP_STAGE_SPECS`. Six (`filings_history_seed`, `sec_submissions_files_walk`, `sec_form3_ingest`, `sec_13f_quarterly_sweep`, `sec_n_port_ingest`, `sec_n_csr_bootstrap_drain`) stayed live in `_INVOKERS` (steady-state safety-net / `sec_rebuild` / Admin "Run now") but their ONLY source-registry path was the dropped bootstrap stage.
- Symptom: `app/jobs/sources.py::_build_job_name_to_source` resolves a job's lane from `SCHEDULED_JOBS ∪ _BOOTSTRAP_STAGE_SPECS ∪ MANUAL_TRIGGER_JOB_SOURCES`. Dropping a stage removes that path; an in-`_INVOKERS` job not covered by the other two → `source_for()` KeyErrors at the next `JobLock` acquisition (steady-state / manual), and `test_source_for_returns_correct_lane` / `test_internal_keys_keys_are_known_jobs` fail.
- Prevention: when dropping a stage from `_BOOTSTRAP_STAGE_SPECS`, for each dropped `job_name` still in `_INVOKERS` and NOT in `SCHEDULED_JOBS`, re-home it into `MANUAL_TRIGGER_JOB_SOURCES` at its correct lane. A re-home to the WRONG lane raises `JobSourceRegistryError` at boot (different-lane collision) — pinned at test time so it fails in CI, not at operator boot.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/jobs/sources.py::MANUAL_TRIGGER_JOB_SOURCES` (#1413 re-homed entries); `tests/test_job_registry.py::TestRegistryConflictDetection::test_real_registry_has_no_conflicting_lane_duplicates` (positive invariant: every multi-registered job_name resolves to exactly one lane).
- Verification step: after dropping any bootstrap stage, `set(_INVOKERS) - set(get_job_name_to_source())` must contain no job you intend to keep invokable; the conflict-detection test stays green.

### Tests pinned to a specific scheduled-job registry entry break when the registry is re-homed

- First seen in: 2026-06-01 — #1413 (bulk-only bootstrap) moved `sec_13f_quarterly_sweep` + `sec_form3_ingest` out of `SCHEDULED_JOBS` into the manual-trigger surface. Three tests that hard-coded those names as scheduled processes silently went red — `test_get_process_returns_params_metadata_for_declared_job` (404), `test_mixed_covered_and_uncovered_failed_rows_stays_failed` (`get_row` None), `test_params_metadata_surfaces_for_sec_13f_quarterly_sweep` (`StopIteration` on the registry lookup). Undetected because CI runs no pytest (removed 2026-05-05); the local pre-push hook is the sole pytest gate and #1413 evidently pushed past it.
- Symptom: a capability test ("a scheduled job that declares `params_metadata` surfaces it on the row/endpoint") asserts the capability THROUGH one named registry entry. When a later PR re-homes that entry, the test fails for a reason unrelated to the capability — and worse, the capability may end up with NO live example (post-#1413 no scheduled job declares `params_metadata` at all), so there is nothing to re-point at.
- Prevention: a test that pins a generic capability (forwarding, surfacing, validation) MUST NOT couple to a specific churnable registry entry unless the test's whole point is that entry's identity. Inject a SYNTHETIC `ScheduledJob` via `monkeypatch.setattr(scheduled_adapter, "SCHEDULED_JOBS", (*..., synth))` (and, for the endpoint path, `monkeypatch.setattr(processes_api, "VALID_JOB_NAMES", ... | {synth.name})`) so the contract is exercised independent of registry churn. For freshness-coverage tests, target a job whose lane/source mapping is intrinsic to the test's scenario, not incidental. When a PR drops/re-homes a registry entry, grep tests for the literal job name before merging.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/test_scheduled_adapter.py::test_params_metadata_surfaces_for_scheduled_job_with_declaration` (synthetic job); `tests/test_processes_endpoints.py::test_get_process_returns_params_metadata_for_declared_job` (synthetic job + patched `VALID_JOB_NAMES`); `tests/test_processes_endpoints.py::test_mixed_covered_and_uncovered_failed_rows_stays_failed` (retargeted to `sec_filing_documents_ingest`/`sec_form4`).
- Verification step: `grep -rn "sec_13f_quarterly_sweep\|sec_form3_ingest" tests/` returns no test asserting either as a *scheduled* process; the three tests above stay green on a clean DB.

### Tests that mutate global pg_database state deadlock under xdist unless serialized

- First seen in: #1444 (2026-06-02). `test_orphan_sweep.py` and the new `test_dev_test_db_reaper.py` both `CREATE`/`DROP`/`UPDATE pg_database` on globally-named databases AND invoke the global orphan sweep (which drops every old-epoch `ebull_test_*`). `CREATE DATABASE` / `DROP DATABASE` take cluster-wide locks; run concurrently across `-n 4` xdist workers they deadlock — three workers wedged ~40 min on mutual `DROP` of the same fixed-name DBs, stalling the whole pre-push full suite. A `datconnlimit=-2` corpse one test marked then blocked another worker's drop; clearing it ultimately needed a PG container recreate (which re-paid the entrypoint chown walk + a WAL redo — see data-engineer SKILL §14).
- Symptom: the full suite (new-branch path, no testmon scope) appears "slow" but is actually wedged — `pg_stat_activity` shows multiple `DROP DATABASE` on the same name with `pg_blocking_pids` cycles; `lock_blocked` climbs; ages exceed minutes. Tests pass in isolation (single worker) so the bug hides until the parallel full run.
- Prevention (two layers):
  1. **Serialize** every test that issues `CREATE`/`DROP DATABASE` or mutates `pg_database`, or invokes a cluster-wide sweep, onto ONE xdist worker: `pytestmark = pytest.mark.xdist_group("<shared-group>")` at module scope (with `--dist=loadgroup`, already in pyproject). All such files MUST share the SAME group string so they serialise against each other, not just within a file. For #1444 the group is `reaper_db_ops` across `test_orphan_sweep.py` + `test_dev_test_db_reaper.py`.
  2. **Prefer pure policy over real DBs.** A reaper/sweep's decision ("given catalog rows + clock, which DBs qualify?") is a pure function — extract it (`app/db/dev_test_db_reaper.py::select_orphans_to_drop`) and unit-test every rail combination DB-free (microseconds, parallel-safe, deadlock-proof). Keep exactly ONE real-DB integration test for the SQL mechanism. This is the calculated/minimal-work-max-coverage shape: ~7 pure tests + 1 serialized integration replaced ~14 real-`CREATE`/`DROP DATABASE` tests with the same coverage and no wedge risk. Migration-replay tests (`run_migrations` into `ebull_mig*`) fall under layer 1 — they also create real databases and belong in the serialized group or a dedicated one.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/test_dev_test_db_reaper.py` (pure rail tests + single `xdist_group("reaper_db_ops")` integration); `tests/test_orphan_sweep.py` (module `pytestmark` group pin); `app/db/dev_test_db_reaper.py::select_orphans_to_drop` (pure policy extracted from `_do_sweep`).
- Verification step: `grep -rn 'CREATE DATABASE\|DROP DATABASE\|run_migrations' tests/ -l` — every hit must carry an `xdist_group` pin (module `pytestmark` or per-test marker) OR be a pure test that never connects. Self-review prompt: a new test that touches `pg_database`/`pg_stat_activity` globally is a serialise-or-make-pure decision before merge.

### Unescaped `_` in a LIKE pattern silently widens the match

- First seen in: #1446 bot review (2026-06-02). A new `pg_database` probe used `datname LIKE 'ebull_test_%'`. In SQL `LIKE`, `_` is a single-char WILDCARD, so the pattern also matches `ebullXtestYanything` — wider than the intended literal `ebull_test_` prefix. The sibling `force_drop_invalid_test_dbs` / `cleanup_test_dbs` already escape it (`LIKE 'ebull!_test!_%' ESCAPE '!'`); the new probe drifted.
- Symptom: a database/table/identifier whose name happens to have any char where the pattern has a literal `_` is wrongly included — here a non-test DB could be counted as a leaked test DB (or, on a destructive sweep, wrongly targeted). The bug is invisible until such a name exists.
- Prevention: any `LIKE` whose pattern contains a `_` that is meant LITERALLY MUST use `ESCAPE` (repo convention: `'..!_..' ESCAPE '!'`). Self-review + the existing escaped sibling are the reference. Grep new SQL for `LIKE '[^']*_` and confirm each `_` is intentionally a wildcard; if literal, escape it.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/postgres_health.py::_q_leaked_test_dbs` + `::_q_leaked_test_db_bytes` (both now `ESCAPE '!'`); reference impls `app/db/dev_test_db_reaper.py::force_drop_invalid_test_dbs` + `tests/fixtures/cleanup_test_dbs.py`.

### Per-row autocommit upsert loop is not atomic — a mid-loop crash freezes a partial set

- First seen in: #1531 bot review (2026-06-08, BLOCKING). C6's `job_first_seen` seed loop in `JobRuntime.start` ran one `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (job_name) DO NOTHING` per job on an `autocommit=True` connection. Each row committed independently, so a crash partway through the loop leaves SOME jobs anchored and others not. Because the loop reruns every boot and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` preserves whatever already exists, the jobs that missed the crashed boot get their anchor stamped at a LATER boot's `now()` — a silently-wrong first-seen timestamp (the never-started grace window starts late), not a fail-open.
- Symptom: a "seed once, idempotent" startup loop looks safe under `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, but per-row autocommit means the batch is not all-or-nothing. The defect is invisible until a crash interleaves with the loop, and then it's a quiet data-correctness drift (wrong anchor timestamp), not a crash.
- Prevention: a startup/seed loop that writes a RELATED SET of rows whose values must be mutually consistent (a shared timestamp, a shared epoch) MUST run in ONE transaction — `with psycopg.connect(..., autocommit=True) as conn, conn.transaction():` (psycopg3 `transaction()` issues an explicit BEGIN/COMMIT even on an autocommit connection). Per-row autocommit is only acceptable when each row is genuinely independent AND a partial set is genuinely harmless. Self-review prompt: "if this loop dies on iteration k, is the persisted prefix a correct state?" If no, wrap it.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/jobs/runtime.py::JobRuntime.start` (`job_first_seen` seed now wrapped in `conn.transaction()`, all anchors share one boot `now()`).
- Verification step: grep startup/seed loops for `autocommit=True` connections that `conn.execute(INSERT ...)` inside a `for`; confirm either a `conn.transaction()` wrapper or an explicit comment that partial application is safe.

### Extracting a job from a shared concurrency lane can expose a write race the serialization was masking

- First seen in: #1534 (2026-06-08), Codex checkpoint-2. To fix `:00` starvation, `sec_per_cik_poll` was moved off the shared `sec_rate` `JobLock` lane into its own lane — so it now runs CONCURRENTLY with the other `sec_rate` discovery producers (`sec_atom_fast_lane`, daily-index) that also write `data_freshness_index`. `record_poll_outcome` updated the watermark with a blind `last_known_filed_at = COALESCE(EXCLUDED.last_known_filed_at, existing)` — last-writer-wins. A per-CIK poll snapshots an old watermark, fetches for seconds, and on "no new filing" writes back its stale snapshot; if a concurrent producer advanced the watermark in that window, the stale write REGRESSED it (un-discovering a filing). While both jobs shared the lane they serialized, so the interleave was impossible — the lane was load-bearing for correctness, not just throughput.
- Symptom: a lane split / de-serialization is framed as anti-starvation or throughput and looks safe because the moved job's write targets already have other writers (so "disjointness" per #1527 appears satisfied). But the shared lane was the only thing preventing two writers from interleaving on the same row. After the split a non-monotonic upsert (blind `COALESCE`, no `GREATEST`/`CASE` guard, no `ON CONFLICT` identity dedup) silently regresses state when a stale and a fresh write race. Invisible until two formerly-serialized writers actually overlap in production; often self-correcting next cycle, so it hides as intermittent churn rather than a hard failure.
- Prevention: #1527's "verify write-target disjointness before extraction" is necessary but NOT sufficient — sharing a table is fine ONLY IF the writes are ORDERING-SAFE: idempotent `ON CONFLICT` keyed by row identity, OR a monotonic `CASE`/`GREATEST` guard that never regresses (watermarks, `max`-seen, `last_*`), OR a per-row `pg_advisory_xact_lock`. Before changing a `ScheduledJob.source` / adding a Lane, for EACH table the moved job writes that another lane member also writes, prove a stale↔fresh interleave cannot corrupt the row. Grep the moved job's write helpers for `COALESCE(EXCLUDED.<col>` on monotonic columns — a blind `COALESCE` is last-writer-wins and must become a regression-guarded `CASE`. Self-review prompt: "this job used to serialize with X through the lane; now it doesn't — can their writes to the same row interleave destructively?"
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/data_freshness.py::record_poll_outcome` (monotonic `CASE` guard on `last_known_filing_id`/`last_known_filed_at`; `expected_next_at` tied to the winning watermark via `>=` so a strictly-stale poll cannot leave the row too-soon-due while the common no-new-data re-poll still advances its cadence). Reference monotonic pattern: the discovery seed-path guard in the same file. Test: `tests/test_data_freshness.py::TestRecordPollOutcome::test_stale_poll_does_not_regress_watermark`.
- Verification step: when a PR changes a `ScheduledJob.source` or adds a `Lane` member, `grep -n "COALESCE(EXCLUDED" app/services/<moved-job-write-helpers>` and confirm every monotonic column is regression-guarded, not blind-COALESCE'd, for each table shared with the lane the job left. A lane split with any unguarded shared-table watermark write is not done.

### Cleanup-path ordering — clear ownership flags AFTER the released-side operation, never before or in a finally

- First seen in: #1543 bot review (2026-06-09, WARNING). `JobLock.__exit__`'s new sec_rate branch did `self._sec_lane_held = False` BEFORE `sec_lane_gate.SEC_LANE_GATE.release(self._job_name)`. If `release()` raised, the flag was already cleared — caller / diagnostics could not see that we still held a gate slot. The bot also flagged that wrapping the release in `try/finally: flag = False` is the same anti-pattern, since `finally` still clears the flag on the exception path.
- Symptom: any cleanup path (`__exit__`, `close`, manual teardown) that has an "I own X" flag PLUS a release call. Clearing the flag before — or in a `finally` around — the release call masks a release-time bug: the resource is leaked AND the held-state is invisible at the moment of the raise. The flag's job is to surface ownership; clearing it speculatively defeats that.
- Prevention: release the resource FIRST; clear the flag only on success (linear, no `try/finally`). If release raises, the exception propagates AND the flag stays True, so the leak signal is preserved for the operator / a retry / a test. Same principle as never setting `committed = True` before `conn.commit()` returns.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/python-hygiene.md` ("Cleanup-path ordering" section); `app/jobs/locks.py::JobLock.__exit__` sec_rate release block.
- Verification step: before pushing any cleanup path with an ownership flag, grep the file for `self\._.*_held\s*=\s*False` and confirm the assignment appears AFTER (not before, not in a `finally` around) the corresponding `release` / `close` / `unlock` call. Same rule for any local `released = True` style flag.

### An allow-list / classification set must cover every naming convention the ingest path accepts — before any downstream delete keys on it

- First seen in: #1013 (2026-06-09), Codex checkpoint-1 + pre-flight grep. The #1011 SEC form allow-list (`SEC_PARSE_AND_RAW` in `app/services/filings.py`) listed only the long-form `SCHEDULE 13D` / `SCHEDULE 13G` names. SEC renamed `SC 13D/G` → `SCHEDULE 13D/G` on 2024-12-19, so the dev DB carries BOTH conventions; the blockholder parser (`app/services/blockholders.py::_SUBMISSIONS_INDEX_FORMS`) explicitly accepts both. The #1013 cleanup job deletes `filing_events` rows whose `filing_type` is NOT in the keep-set — a literal sweep against the incomplete keep-set would have deleted **131,545** legacy short-form 13D/G rows that an active parser consumes. A documentation oversight in a constant became 131k-row data loss the instant a delete keyed on it.
- Symptom: an enum / allow-list / form-type set is frozen from one source's naming (a spec table, one feed's labels, today's data) while a different ingest path accepts a superset of names (legacy aliases, a renamed code, a second feed's labels). Nothing breaks while the set is only used for *filtering-in* (the missing names just don't get the bonus treatment). The latent gap detonates when something keys a *delete / exclusion / downgrade* on "NOT IN the set" — the un-enumerated-but-real rows are now actively destroyed, not merely skipped.
- Prevention: the durable guard is to couple the set to the producer's own accepted-name set with a superset test, NOT to re-list names by hand. Before freezing an allow-list that a delete/exclusion will key on, grep every ingest/parser path that writes the column for its accepted-value set (`grep -n "filing_type IN\|filing_type =\|_FORMS\|_INDEX_FORMS" app/services/`), and assert `keep_set ⊇ ⋃(parser accepted-sets)` in a pure-logic test that imports the parser's actual constant. If a parser learns a new name, the test fails until the keep-set covers it — the set can never silently drop below what a parser consumes. Belt-and-braces for a one-shot destructive run: an empirical preflight (candidate-to-delete keys ∩ parser-owned tables = ∅) recorded in the PR. Do NOT build a runtime cross-check against every parser table into the job — that couples a one-shot to all parsers; the structural test is the right altitude.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (§ allow-list / form-type discipline); `app/services/filings.py::SEC_PARSE_AND_RAW` (4 short-form 13D/G aliases added, cross-referencing `blockholders.py`); `tests/test_filings_form_allowlist.py::test_keep_set_covers_blockholder_accepted_forms` (the superset guard).
- Verification step: when adding/changing a form-type or category allow-list that any DELETE / `NOT IN` / exclusion predicate reads, confirm a pure-logic superset test couples it to each producing parser's accepted-value constant. A keep-set that lists names by hand with no producer-coupled test is one rename away from silent data loss.

### Migration content drift after the schema_migrations row is recorded

- First seen in: #1333 (2026-05-24, dev-DB RCA on PR #1332's `column "last_jobs_boot_error_at" of relation "bootstrap_state" does not exist` jobs-boot WARN). An early draft of `sql/171` was applied to dev during PR development; the merged version added a second column + CHECK constraint. `app/db/migrations.py` keyed "already applied" on filename only, so re-running migrations silently skipped the changed file and the DB stayed permanently behind the merged migration.
- Symptom: editing an applied `sql/NNN_*.sql` (during PR development, review fixes, or rebase) is a silent no-op on every DB that already recorded the row. Surface symptom is a downstream "column/constraint X does not exist" error or WARN that looks like fresh-clone-vs-incremental drift, hours-to-days later, on a path unrelated to the migration.
- Prevention: ALL content changes to an already-applied migration must go into a new `NNN+1` file. The runner enforces this since #1333: `schema_migrations.content_sha256` stores each applied file's hash; a mismatch on a later boot raises `RuntimeError` (fail-fast at lifespan, not a WARN) with the bump-or-replay instructions in the message. Legacy NULL-hash rows are backfilled one-time from current content.
- Enforced in: `app/db/migrations.py::run_migrations` (drift check + backfill); `tests/test_migration_content_drift.py` (apply-records-sha / drift-raises / backfill-idempotent).
- Verification step: if you knowingly edited an applied migration on a dev DB (idempotent manual replay), reset its row with `UPDATE schema_migrations SET content_sha256 = NULL WHERE filename = '<file>'` AFTER replaying — the next boot re-records the hash. Never delete the row (that would re-run the whole file).

### Frontend spy call-history leaks across tests without per-file `vi.restoreAllMocks` (exposed by vitest 4 spy reuse)

- First seen in: #1417 (2026-06-10, vite 5→6 + vitest 2→4 toolchain upgrade). 3 tests in `FilingsPane.test.tsx` + `EightKListPage.test.tsx` failed only under vitest 4: `spy.mock.calls[0]` returned the PREVIOUS test's call, and `not.toHaveBeenCalled()` / `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2)` counted prior-test calls.
- Symptom: vitest 4 returns the SAME spy when `vi.spyOn` re-targets an already-spied method, so call history accumulates across tests in a file. Under vitest 2 the suite was green only because spy semantics masked the missing isolation — the tests were latently order-dependent. Repo-wide config keeps `clearMocks`/`restoreMocks` OFF, so isolation is each file's job.
- Prevention: any test file asserting on spy call history (`mock.calls[...]`, `not.toHaveBeenCalled()`, `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(n)`) for a `vi.spyOn` target must register a module-scope `afterEach(() => { vi.restoreAllMocks(); })`. Grep on adding such assertions: `grep -L "restoreAllMocks" $(grep -rl "mock.calls\|toHaveBeenCalledTimes\|not.toHaveBeenCalled" frontend/src --include="*.test.tsx")`.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/test-quality.md` (Mock discipline § frontend `vi.spyOn`); `frontend/src/components/instrument/FilingsPane.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/EightKListPage.test.tsx`.

---

### `type: ignore[arg-type]` masking a psycopg Connection row-factory mismatch
- First seen in: PR #1583
- Symptom: `reconcile_symbol_history(conn)  # type: ignore[arg-type]` silenced a `Connection[tuple]` vs unparameterised-connection mismatch. With a dict_row caller, the function's `int(r[0])` fetches would KeyError at runtime — the ignore traded a compile-time error for a latent runtime one.
- Prevention: never `type: ignore[arg-type]` a psycopg Connection argument. Either the function genuinely depends on the row shape — then open its own cursor with an explicit `row_factory=psycopg.rows.tuple_row` (or `dict_row`) and widen the signature to `Connection[Any]` — or it only uses `rowcount`/`execute`, in which case `Connection[Any]` alone is honest.
- Enforced in: `.claude/skills/engineering/python-hygiene.md` (psycopg typing section)

### `new Date()` / `Date.now()` computed in a render body and fed into async-hook dep arrays

- PR #1586 review WARNING: `windowFromDate(window, new Date())` evaluated every render, result in `useAsync` deps. Benign THERE only because the helper returns a date STRING (value-stable within a day) — the same pattern returning a `Date` object or epoch ms refetches on every parent re-render.
- Rule: any `new Date()` / `Date.now()` whose result reaches a `useAsync` / `useEffect` / `useCallback` dep array must be wrapped in `useMemo` keyed on the inputs that should drive recomputation. Stability must be explicit, not incidental to a string return type.

### Category folds must propagate to every category-keyed consumer (export filters, deep links)

- First seen in: PR #1595 (#1589 Codex ckpt-2 P1).
- Symptom: the ownership chart + L2 filer table fold `def14a_unmatched` into the `insiders` category (`rollupToSunburstInputs` / `rollupToFilerRows`), but the CSV export endpoint filtered `?category=insiders` by exact slice match — a drilled `?category=insiders&view=raw` export silently dropped DEF 14A rows the filtered table visibly showed. The fold lived in the FE transform; the backend consumer keyed on the raw category never learned about it.
- Prevention: when a surface folds category A into category B for display, grep every consumer keyed on category (`?category=` filters, CSV/export endpoints, deep-link params, history filters) and apply the same fold — or route them all through one shared fold function. Self-review prompt: for each new `SLICE_TO_*` / fold map, list the category-keyed entry points (`grep -rn "category" app/api frontend/src/api` scoped to the feature) and confirm each either folds identically or is exact-by-design with a comment.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/api/instruments.py::rollup_csv_slice_filter` (pure helper + table test in `tests/test_ownership_rollup_csv.py`).

---

### Non-abstract ABC default methods that raise must be caught by every safety wrapper around the call

- First seen in: PR #1610 (#1593 review round 3 WARNING).
- Symptom: `BrokerProvider.get_trade_history` ships a non-abstract default that raises `NotImplementedError` (kept non-abstract so existing test fakes don't break). The degrade-gracefully wrapper `fetch_trade_history_safely` caught `httpx` errors + parse errors but not `NotImplementedError` — a future provider without an override would escape the wrapper and fail the whole position sync, the exact outcome the wrapper exists to prevent.
- Prevention: when an interface method's default implementation raises (NotImplementedError or otherwise), every "this call is allowed to fail" wrapper around it must include that exception class in its catch set, with a loud log naming the provider class. Self-review prompt: for each `except (...)` wrapper around a provider/interface call, read the BASE class implementation — if it can raise something the wrapper misses, add it.
- Enforced in: `app/services/trade_events.py::fetch_trade_history_safely` (`except NotImplementedError` branch) + the parametrized error-posture table in `tests/test_trade_events.py`.

### `get_runtime_config` (and any config read) must share the snapshot of the queries that consume it

- First seen in: PR #1622 (#1594 PR-B review WARNING).
- Symptom: `get_value_history` called `get_runtime_config(conn)` to read `display_currency` BEFORE the `with snapshot_read(conn):` block. `snapshot_read` commits the pending implicit transaction on entry, so the config read landed at an earlier snapshot than the positions / FX / persisted-overlay queries. A mid-request `display_currency` change would read the new currency for the queries but the old one for the overlay filter (or vice-versa), mismatching the `portfolio_eod_snapshots` display-ccy gate against data read at a different snapshot.
- Prevention: any value that parameterises a multi-statement read (display currency, feature flags, thresholds from `runtime_config`) MUST be read INSIDE the same `snapshot_read` block as the queries that consume it — never before it. `snapshot_read`'s entry-commit means a pre-block read is a *different* snapshot. Self-review prompt: grep `get_runtime_config(` / other config reads in any handler that uses `snapshot_read`; confirm the read is inside the block, not above it.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/api/portfolio.py::get_value_history` (config read moved inside `snapshot_read`).

---

### A server-driven empty/`no_data` state usually has MORE THAN ONE hardcoded FE surface — fix every one

- First seen in: PR #1624 (#1581 — Codex ckpt-1 F1 + ckpt-2 F1 caught the SAME class twice in one session).
- Symptom: the ownership rollup `no_data` state is rendered by TWO hardcoded empty-states — `OwnershipPanel.tsx` (L1) and `OwnershipPage.tsx` (L2) — each with its own "Trigger a fundamentals sync" copy. The stale-denominator guard made the server banner honest, but fixing only the L1 panel left the L2 page contradicting it (honest banner above, "sync" copy below). The author asserted "no FE change" reasoning purely from the banner being server-verbatim, then fixed one of two empty-states.
- Prevention: when you change what a server-driven state (`no_data` / `error` / empty) MEANS or how it should read, grep EVERY surface that branches on it before claiming done — there is usually a panel AND a page (and sometimes a CSV/export). `grep -rn 'banner.state === "<state>"' frontend/src` + grep the hardcoded copy string. Route the new per-state copy through ONE shared helper returning `string | null` (each surface keeps its own default) rather than duplicating the literal — duplication drifts. "Grep for absence": asserting "no FE change" requires grepping every consumer of the changed state, not reasoning from one file.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/frontend/loading-error-empty-states.md` ("Multi-surface states share one copy source"); `frontend/src/components/instrument/ownershipMetrics.ts::ownershipStaleDenominatorCopy` (single source, consumed by both `OwnershipPanel` + `OwnershipPage`).

### A multi-row source position must be SUM-aggregated at EVERY ingest locus — with an identical key + secondary-field derivation across paths

- First seen in: PR for #1567/#1566 (Codex ckpt-1 caught the bulk path; the issue had scoped it as "verify only").
- Symptom: a 13F-HR legitimately splits ONE `(cusip, putCall)` position across multiple infotable rows by `otherManager` / discretion. FOUR ingest paths (legacy first-ingest, manifest worker, CUSIP rewash, bulk DERA drain) each collapsed those rows keep-one instead of summing — a 10.3% undercount on Vanguard's AAPL position (7 rows summing 1,426,283,914 stored as the single 1,279,051,701 SOLE row). Three paths used a keep-first `setdefault`; the bulk path used `SELECT DISTINCT ON (...) ORDER BY ctid DESC` (keep-last). The bug was invisible per-path because each "looked deduped". Voting authority was ALSO wrong: keep-one recorded one row's label, but the correct aggregate compares the SUMMED sole/shared/none sub-amounts.
- Prevention: when a source row is a *component* of a position (sub-manager splits, multi-lot, per-account tranches), it must be SUM-aggregated at EVERY locus that writes the position — Python per-row paths AND the bulk COPY/SQL drain. Three rules: (1) aggregate, don't dedupe — `DISTINCT ON` / `setdefault` / `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` keep ONE row and silently drop the rest; use a shared aggregator (Python helper for per-row paths, `GROUP BY ... SUM` for the bulk drain). (2) The aggregation KEY must be identical across paths (here `(instrument_id, exposure)`); a per-filing path keyed on `cusip` and a bulk path keyed on `instrument_id` diverge on aliased identifiers — add a post-resolution merge so they match. (3) A derived secondary field (voting authority) must be SUM-then-derive over its components, derived ONCE by one rule — staging a pre-derived per-row label and picking one loses the aggregate; the bulk SQL CASE must mirror the Python derivation (`dominant_voting_authority`) exactly, pinned by a parity test. Self-review prompt: list every writer of the position table/observation (grep the upsert/COPY targets); for each, confirm it SUMs components, keys identically, and derives secondary fields from summed components.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` ("Multi-row source positions: SUM at every locus"); `app/services/thirteen_f_normalise.py` (shared `normalise_13f_holdings` + `merge_resolved_by_instrument`); `app/services/sec_13f_dataset_ingest.py::_INSERT_FROM_STG_SQL` (`GROUP BY ... SUM` + voting CASE); `tests/test_13f_normalise_applied_everywhere.py` (superset guard) + `tests/test_thirteen_f_normalise.py::test_dominant_voting_matches_bulk_sql_case` (voting parity).

### Same owner across reporting channels: MAX overlapping restatements, SUM only additive natures

- First seen in: PR for #1640 (Codex ckpt-2 F3 caught it; the rollup already summed Form 4 + matched DEF 14A for one director, a latent double-count predating this PR).
- Symptom: ownership concentration / residual sum every pie-wedge slice total, so a single beneficial owner reported through multiple channels was counted more than once. Two layers: (a) CROSS-source — an insider's Form 4 and his 13D, or a manager's 13G and 13F, are the SAME stake through two lenses and were both summed (Cohen on GME: 8.55% insider + 8.21% blockholder = 48.37% known vs truthful 40.16%). (b) WITHIN-source — DEF 14A `beneficial` and `voting` rows for one holder (the `ownership_*_current` PK admits both) were summed, double-counting the owner inside the insiders slice. The first naive fix (MAX across sources, SUM within source) FIXED (a) but left (b), because "SUM within source" is only correct for *additive* natures.
- Prevention: a beneficial owner is ONE unit of account (Rule 13d-3), counted ONCE at their total beneficial ownership, classified by most-specific role (Section-16 insider > 13F institution > 13D/G blockholder) — never by which form they filed or how the name string reads. Aggregation has TWO regimes that must not be conflated: **additive** natures (Form 4 / Form 3 `direct` + `indirect` are distinct Section-16 holdings → SUM, the #905 rule) vs **overlapping** restatements (DEF 14A beneficial/voting, a 13D/G beneficial figure, a 13F economic position, and the same owner's figures across channels → all the SAME shares → MAX, never SUM). The canonical reference is the SEC proxy "Security Ownership of Certain Beneficial Owners and Management" table: a director who is also a 5% holder appears on ONE line at their total beneficial ownership. Self-review prompt: whenever you aggregate per-owner figures, ask "are these distinct holdings (SUM) or the same shares restated (MAX)?" — and check it BOTH within a source's nature rows and across sources for the same identity.
  - **(#1941 follow-on) The additive SUM must span the additive SOURCES, not just the natures inside one source.** `ownership_insiders_current`'s PK is `(instrument, holder, nature)`, so a person's latest `direct` can carry `source=form4` while their latest `indirect` carries `source=form3` (Form 3 = initial-holdings snapshot, Form 4 = subsequent transactions; `source` is a per-nature provenance stamp, not a channel boundary). The read path bucketed `by_source`, SUMmed additive natures *within* each form, then MAXed the two form subtotals — dropping the smaller form's lot (AAPL / Khan Sabih: `direct` 1,073,895 F4 + `indirect` 31,632 F3 → shown 1,073,895, true 1,105,527). Full-pop: 2,859 groups / 1,468 instruments, ~2.06B insider shares undercounted. Fix: pool Form 3 + Form 4 into ONE Section-16 additive channel (latest row per nature across both forms, then SUM) before the cross-source MAX. Lesson: when the additive regime is defined by a set of SOURCES (`_ADDITIVE_SOURCES`), a per-source loop silently re-imposes a channel boundary the natures were meant to cross — pool the additive sources first.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` ("One beneficial owner, counted once — MAX overlapping, SUM additive"); `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_reconcile_owner_once` + `_source_rows_and_total` + `_merge_section16_forms` (pools `{form4, form3}` into one additive channel, #1941); `tests/test_ownership_owner_reconcile.py` (`test_def14a_beneficial_and_voting_not_summed`, `test_13f_managed_assets_never_added_to_insider`, `test_source_rows_and_total_helper`, `test_form3_indirect_plus_form4_direct_summed_not_maxed`, `test_section16_pooled_forms_then_max_against_def14a`, `test_section16_same_nature_across_forms_latest_wins_not_summed`).

### A 13F-NT (Notice) supersedes the filer's prior 13F-HR — model it by `period_end`, never `filed_at`

- First seen in: PR for #1639 (Codex ckpt-1 HIGH #1 caught the filed_at axis; the live Vanguard reorg falsified the naive design).
- Symptom: when a 13F filer restructures (parent declares "nothing reportable" via a **13F-NT Notice**, its book moving to spun-out sub-entity CIKs that each file their own 13F-HR), our pipeline — which ingests 13F-HR only, with 13F-NT deliberately absent from `_FORM_TO_SOURCE` — never learned the parent's prior HR was dead. The rollup summed the parent's stale quarter alongside the sub-entities' post-reorg quarter, ~2× double-counting (Vanguard on AAPL: 2.86B sh / 19.5% vs the real ~9.8%). A 13F-HR is a quarterly *snapshot*; "latest per filer in `_current`" keeps a superseded parent because nothing tombstones it. SEC's NT *is* the anti-double-count primitive — but it is a separate form we dropped at discovery.
- Prevention: capture 13F-NT filings (form ∈ {13F-NT, 13F-NT/A}) into a standalone table and exclude a filer's HR from the rollup when that filer filed an NT for a **later quarter** (`NT.period_end > HR.period_end`). The axis MUST be `period_end`, NOT `filed_at`: a `13F-NT/A` amending an *old* quarter can be filed *after* a filer has resumed holdings reporting — its `filed_at` is later than the live HR's, but its period is older, so a filed-time comparison would wrongly suppress the live HR. Strict `>` keeps the HR on an equal/contradictory same-quarter period (never drop holdings on non-strictly-later evidence). Derive the NT's `filer_cik` from the primary_doc header `<cik>` (the first `<cik>` — an NT lists every *other* manager's CIK after it) so it joins identically to the HR-derived `ownership_institutions_current.filer_cik`. Self-review prompt: for any "latest snapshot per filer" read, ask "is there a SEC mechanism that *retires* a prior snapshot (an NT, a withdrawal, a tombstone) that I am not modelling?" — and when you model supersession across amendments, compare on the *period* axis, not file-time.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (invariant I15 + "13F-NT supersession" note + FAQ Q1); `sql/199_institutional_filer_13f_notices.sql`; `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_read_notice_suppressions` + the `NOT EXISTS` clause in `_collect_canonical_holders_from_current`; `app/services/sec_13f_notice_sync.py`; `tests/test_ownership_13f_nt_supersession.py` (parametrised period-axis cases incl `nt_a_old_quarter`), `tests/test_sec_13f_notice_parser.py`, `tests/test_sec_13f_notice_sync.py`.

### Proxy 5%-holder is a family-consolidated restatement, not an independent additive wedge — count manager families once at MAX across channels

- First seen in: PR for #1644 + #1649 (the data-trust committee scoped #1644 as a pure proxy double-count; dev evidence falsified that framing and merged it with #1649).
- Symptom: the DEF 14A "Security Ownership of Certain Beneficial Owners" 5%-holder rows for institutional managers ("The Vanguard Group", "BlackRock, Inc.") were added to the rollup as an **additive `def14a_unmatched` pie wedge** on top of the 13F institutions slice. The committee called it a uniform ~2× double-count. Dev (AAPL) showed it is TWO opposite bugs with one root cause — missing institutional family identity: Vanguard's proxy 1.415B duplicated the summed 13F sub-CIKs (1.436B) → real double-count; but BlackRock's proxy 1.043B was the ONLY complete figure (its 13F captured a 6.1M shell; the real iShares/BFA sub-book CIK is absent from ingestion) → dropping it would have deleted the truth. Both naive fixes failed: resolve-proxy-name→single-CIK (the proxy is a *family-consolidated* 13G figure under Reg S-K Item 403 — no single CIK equals it; names don't even normalise-match, e.g. `"The\xa0Vanguard\xa0Group"` vs `"VANGUARD GROUP INC"`); drop-institutional-named-rows (kills BlackRock's only figure). Whether a proxy row double-counts depends on whether the family's 13F is captured — which is exactly the family-aggregation question (#1649). The same data also carried DEF 14A parser-garbage share values (LAMR `Kevin Reilly` = 48.3 *trillion* shares, ~300,000× outstanding) that an additive wedge or a naive MAX would let detonate the rollup.
- Prevention: treat an institutional manager **family** as ONE unit of account, counted once at `MAX(Σ family 13F holdings, family proxy figure, family 13G figure)`. The 13F per-CIK rows are disjoint sub-books that **sum** within the family (post-#1639 NT supersession removes the overlapping parent); the proxy/13G are family-consolidated restatements of the same beneficial block → **MAX** the channels, never sum. MAX-once fixes both directions automatically (proxy folds under a larger 13F sum; or fills the gap a missing sub-book leaves) WITHOUT ingesting the missing CIK. Family identity = a curated, in-code, fail-closed registry (CIK ∪ name-pattern, validated for no cross-family CIK/pattern overlap, ambiguous match → singleton) — deterministic + auditable, NOT auto-detection. The pre-pass MUST run BEFORE the per-CIK owner-once reconciliation (after it, the raw channel figures are already folded into `dropped_sources` and unrecoverable). Sanity-**reject** (never cap — capping fabricates a 100%-owner that still wins MAX) any channel row > shares_outstanding before the MAX, on EVERY channel feeding the figure (proxy AND 13G AND the 13F sum), not just proxy. Preserve the 13F sub-CIK breakdown for display via a non-additive `family_members` field so the collapse stays auditable. Self-review prompt: when a source figure is a *consolidated restatement* of a position reported elsewhere (proxy beneficial table, 13G family roll-up, aggregate disclosures), ask "is this independent ownership or the same block through another lens?" — restatements reconcile-once at MAX against the channel they restate, they are never additive wedges; and a name-keyed additive wedge silently double-counts the moment the same entity is CIK-keyed elsewhere.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (invariant I16); `app/services/institutional_families.py` (registry + `_validate_registry` fail-closed); `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_reconcile_institutional_families` + `_value_is_sane` + `FamilyMember`; spec `docs/specs/etl/2026-06-15-institutional-family-identity.md`; `tests/test_institutional_families.py` (Vanguard fold, BlackRock gap-fill, garbage rejection, singleton passthrough, duplicate-proxy collapse, registry validation).

### Verify the assumed group signal exists in the source before designing the pipeline — a 13D Rule 13d-5 group is counted once in the blockholders wedge

- First seen in: PR for #1645 (the session handoff hypothesised a schema + parser + 13D-rewash-backfill + jobs-restart fix keyed on the parsed `member_of_group` checkbox; grepping the dev raw XML falsified the premise before any code was written).
- Symptom: members of a Schedule 13D/G **group** (Rule 13d-5(b)) each report the IDENTICAL aggregate group stake on separate accessions with distinct reporter CIKs — each member is deemed to beneficially own the whole group's holdings. Per-CIK dedup (`_dedup_within_source`) and per-CIK owner-once both keep all N members, so an N-member group sums N× in the blockholders pie wedge (TKO: 3× ~94M = 282M attributed vs ~94M real, confirmed against the SEC `primary_doc.xml`). Worse, half the dev clusters' members are ALSO Section-16 insiders, so owner-once routes them to the insiders slice — the headline 282M was measured from the raw `_current` table, not the rendered rollup, and the rendered double-count splits across slices.
- Prevention: TWO lessons. (1) **Verify the assumed signal is actually present in the source before designing a pipeline around it.** The `<memberOfGroup>` Item-2 checkbox is ABSENT from the modern (post-2024-12-18) 13D `primary_doc.xml` (`<reportingPersonInfo>` carries only CIK/name/aggregate/type), and where the legacy `blockholder_filings.member_of_group` column is populated it is noise ('a' and 'b' both appear for genuine groups). A `grep` of the stored raw + the column's value distribution collapsed a schema/parser/backfill/restart plan into a **pure read-path** change keyed on a signal already in `_current` (same issuer + same `period_end` + near-equal non-round `aggregate_amount_owned`). A pipeline built on a signal that isn't in the data ships a no-op. (2) **A Rule 13d-5 group is ONE unit of account at MAX**, inferred conservatively (it fails toward keeping holders separate): distinct non-null CIKs; same `period_end`; a **member-count tiered tolerance** — a 2-member numeric coincidence is plausible so demand near-exact (tight 0.001% gate), a 3-way coincidence is negligible so the loose 0.1% band is safe (and catches a control group's personal slivers, e.g. TKO 0.064%); a **round** value (multiple of 100k) never seeds OR joins a cluster (round-lot coincidence — and a round member must not inflate a cluster past the 2-member gate); a member owner-once will reconcile cross-channel (also files Form 4 / 13F / matched DEF 14A) is EXCLUDED (else its other-channel rows orphan and re-count the block). Self-review prompts: before adding a schema column / backfill / parser bump for a "signal", grep the stored raw for the actual element AND the stored column's value distribution — if absent or noise, the signal is elsewhere (or the fix is read-only). When inferring an identity (a group, a family) from numeric coincidence, ask "what is the smallest cluster size where coincidence is plausible?" and put the tight gate there. And scope to what renders cleanly: the Form 4 cross-channel explosion on all-insider control groups (insiders >100% of float) is a separate, larger bug — filed as #1652, not crammed into this fix.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (invariant I17); `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_reconcile_13d_groups` + `_is_collapsible_cluster` + `_is_group_block` + `_collapse_blockholder_group`; spec `docs/specs/etl/2026-06-16-blockholder-13d-group-collapse.md`; `tests/test_blockholder_group_collapse.py` (18 cases incl SKE/RIME/BTAI/3-member collapse, round-lot + round-member count-inflation, 2-member loose-pair, distinct-CIK, survivor-overlap, max-anchored ladder); follow-up #1652 (Form 4 cross-channel explosion).

### A source fact can exist at SEC yet be unreachable through our ingest — verify reachability, not just existence (the companyfacts dimensional-strip)

- First seen in: PR for #1646 (the issue asserted the per-class denominator "exists but isn't selected per-class"; querying the dev DB + live SEC APIs falsified the "selectable" premise before any selection code was written).
- Symptom: for a multi-class issuer whose classes share one SEC CIK (GOOG/GOOGL, HEI/HEI.A), the ownership rollup divided each class's per-class (CUSIP-resolved) holdings by the issuer's COMBINED all-class `shares_outstanding` → both classes understated ~2× (GOOGL institutions rendered 20.97% vs the true 43.7% against Class A). The issue's proposed fix — "pick the per-class `dei:EntityCommonStockSharesOutstanding` for the denominator" — assumed that fact is in `financial_facts_raw`. It is not: `financial_facts_raw` is sourced from the `companyfacts` JSON API, which returns ONLY the non-dimensional ("default") member of each concept. A multi-class issuer reports `EntityCommonStockSharesOutstanding` per class, each tagged with a `us-gaap:StatementClassOfStockAxis` member → no default value → the companyfacts/companyconcept API returns 404/absent for it (Alphabet CIK 1652044 verified). The per-class values exist ONLY in the per-filing inline XBRL instance (`goog-20250331_htm.xml`), which we don't ingest.
- Prevention: TWO lessons. (1) **"The data exists at the source" ≠ "the data is reachable through our pipeline."** Before designing code that *selects* a fact, confirm the fact actually lands in the table you plan to read — grep the ingest path and, for XBRL, remember companyfacts/companyconcept/frames strip ALL dimensional (axis-member) facts (per-class, per-segment, per-coregistrant). Dimensional facts ride on the per-filing instance doc or the DERA Financial Statement Data Sets `num.tsv` `segments` column (#1590), never the JSON APIs. A "just select it" fix built on an absent fact ships a no-op. (2) **When the correct figure isn't ingestible yet, degrade honestly — never fabricate the split.** Detect the condition and surface a server-owned caveat (`dual_class_denominator` with a `note` the FE renders verbatim + an inert CSV memo row) that marks every percentage a combined-basis lower bound; keep the numerators (which ARE per-class-correct). This is NOT a `CorrectionApplied` (it removes no shares). Detection must be precise (no false caveats — a wrong "these are separate share classes" claim is its own fabrication): three gates, all required — the denominator falling back to us-gaap `CommonStockSharesOutstanding` (the multi-class fingerprint: a `dei` denominator means a single non-dimensional cover value → single-class issuer, ETF/ETN trust, or `.US` dual-listing), ≥2 instruments on the canonical CIK, and ≥2 distinct CUSIPs among the siblings (rejects same-security `.US` listing dups). Self-review prompts: before writing "select X from table T", grep that T's writer actually persists X (and that the upstream API doesn't drop it); when the right figure is unavailable, ask "can I make the existing figure HONEST with a caveat?" rather than computing a fabricated approximation; and gate any inferred-identity caveat so precisely that it never fires on a non-instance (here, empirically zero false positives across the dev universe).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-sources/sec-edgar.md` §7.17 (companyfacts dimensional-strip); `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (invariant I18 + FAQ Q16); `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_detect_dual_class_denominator` + `DualClassDenominator` + `_dual_class_note`; `tests/test_ownership_rollup.py::TestDualClassDenominatorDetector` (3-gate cases), `tests/test_ownership_rollup_csv.py` (memo row + note copy); follow-up #1590 (DERA FSDS per-class denominator).

### A snapshot-supersession correction is destructive when ported verbatim onto the matching time-series — the series keeps the axis the snapshot collapsed

- First seen in: PR for #1648 (the issue proposed porting #1639's rollup NT filter into `ownership_history.py`; dev data falsified it before any port was written).
- Symptom: #1639 fixed the rollup **snapshot** by excluding a 13F filer's `_current` HR when it filed a 13F-NT for a LATER quarter (`NT.period_end > HR.period_end`). The snapshot holds ONE row per filer (its latest HR) and has NO time axis, so a reorganised parent's stale latest quarter sums alongside its successors' latest quarter as if simultaneous — the NT filter restores the lost ordering. #1648 asked to apply the same filter to the **history time-series** (`_institutions_aggregate_history` / `_institutions_history`, which read the append-only observations). But the series buckets by `period_end` — it KEEPS the axis. A 13F-HR for period P is a valid-time fact ("filer reported S as of P"); a later NT changes who files *going forward*, it does not retract P. Porting `NT.period_end > obs.period_end` onto the observations suppresses the parent's HR at EVERY quarter earlier than its NT → deletes real history (dev: AAPL aggregate 2025-06-30 4672M→3319M, 2025-12-31 4793M→3363M, ~1.4B sh/qtr erased, because the parent filed one NT for 2026-Q1). The same-period HR+NT collisions that *could* justify a series-level suppression are essentially nonexistent (1 pair in the whole dev DB). Note the snapshot vs series are mirror images of the #1644 family case too: the snapshot double-counts because it collapses the axis; the series self-separates because it preserves it.
- Prevention: before reusing a dedup / supersession / "latest-wins" correction across surfaces, ask "what did the surface I built it for COLLAPSE that the new surface preserves?" A snapshot collapses the time axis (latest-per-key) and the correction exists to compensate for that loss; a period-bucketed series, an as-of history, or an append-only log all KEEP the axis, so the same correction over-applies and destroys valid earlier facts. The honest concern for a time-series is usually coherence / coverage (surface it as facts-not-thresholds, à la #1647), NOT supersession. Pin the rejection with a guard test (the readers' executable body must contain no notices / supersession join, docstring stripped so the *explanation* of the rejection doesn't trip it) so a future agent re-reading "NT-blind" cannot re-introduce the undercount; and dev-verify that the real series VALUES are unchanged (AAPL 2025-06-30 still 4672M, the superseded parent's pre-NT history intact). Self-review prompt: when a correction is "exclude the stale / superseded row," confirm the new surface actually HAS stale rows to exclude — if it indexes by the very axis the staleness was relative to (period, valid-time), the row isn't stale there.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (invariant I15 — "snapshot-only, never ported to the history readers" clause); `app/services/ownership_history.py` (module + `_institutions_aggregate_history` / `_institutions_history` docstrings); `tests/test_ownership_history_coverage.py::TestRejectNtGuard` (docstring-stripped SQL tripwire) + `TestSummariseAggregateCoverage`; `tests/test_ownership_history.py::TestAggregateCoverageEnvelope`; spec `docs/specs/etl/2026-06-16-ownership-history-coverage-coherence.md`; follow-up #1656 (Vanguard parent Q3 defective/partial winner).

### Relaxing a guard mid-PR — update the spec in the SAME change, or it ships contradicting the code

- First seen in: PR #1661 (#1623 per-class denominator). Codex ckpt-1 proposed a strict `class_period_end == outstanding_as_of` period-coherence guard; Codex ckpt-2 + a dev-data finding (companyfacts updates a quarter ahead of FSDS → equality would never fire) relaxed it to the repo's existing 548-day `_denominator_too_stale` bound. The code + tests were updated; the spec doc (added in the SAME PR) still described the strict-equality guard → the review bot caught a BLOCKING spec-vs-impl contradiction on the feature's headline guard.
- Symptom: a guard / contract / threshold is revised partway through a PR (in response to review or a verification finding) and the change lands in code + tests, but the spec section that describes it verbatim is left at the pre-revision wording. A reviewer reads the spec, reads the code, and they disagree — the spec is freshly added in the same diff, so this is not stale drift, it is a same-PR divergence. The danger is asymmetric: the spec is the artifact a future agent trusts to re-derive intent, so a wrong spec mis-teaches every later change.
- Prevention: when you change a guard's signature or any of its conditions, immediately grep the spec (and any prose doc) for the function name and the old condition text, and update every occurrence in the SAME commit — treat the spec section as part of the function's blast radius, not a separate task. Pin the coupling in the function docstring with an explicit "KEEP IN SYNC: <spec path> §N" pointer so the next editor sees the obligation at the edit site. Self-review prompt before pushing: "I changed a guard/contract this PR — does the spec I wrote still describe the version that's in the code, or the version I first proposed?"
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_should_use_class_denominator` ("KEEP IN SYNC" docstring pointer to the spec §7 step 5); `docs/specs/etl/2026-06-17-per-class-shares-denominator.md` §7 step 5 + §9 + §11.2 (reconciled to the relaxed guard).

### A derived aggregate has no independent cross-source — tie out to the primary source, and the denominator is the highest-leverage tie-out

- First seen in: PR (#1647 part 5, ownership denominator cross-check). The committee framed pt5 as "cross-source each ownership figure against an independent reputable source." Research falsified the premise for the NUMERATORS: institutional / insider / blockholder % are *derived* by summing the same SEC 13F/Form 4/13D filings every vendor reads — there is no second source. Two vendors disagree only by method (GOOGL institutions: Fintel 84.99% vs ours 79.78%, identical SEC data, different 13F-NT supersession + family dedup), and EdgarTools' `ThirteenF` is per-manager — no aggregate-issuer-% exists anywhere. Commercial aggregators are runtime-banned (#532) regardless. The DENOMINATOR, by contrast, has independent SEC disclosures (dei cover-page vs us-gaap balance-sheet) AND is the one figure the as-of/coherence + sanity layers structurally cannot validate — #1646's 2× combined-vs-per-class denominator passed every internal check for the ownership card's whole life until a human cross-sourced it.
- Symptom: a "cross-source / validate figure X" requirement is scoped against a figure that has no independent source (a sum/aggregate everyone derives from the same primary filings), so any "second source" is the same data re-aggregated with different conventions — a noisy, methodology-dependent comparison dressed as validation. Meanwhile the figure that IS independently checkable and is the actual silent-failure surface (the shared denominator every percentage divides by) goes unvalidated.
- Prevention: before building a cross-source check, ask "is this figure DERIVED from a primary source everyone shares, or independently disclosed?" A derived aggregate cannot be cross-sourced — validate it by tie-out to the primary source via an INDEPENDENT path (re-derive / a second SEC concept), and document that the aggregate is single-source rather than faking a vendor comparison. Spend the cross-check budget on the highest-leverage, independently-disclosed figure — for ratios that is the DENOMINATOR (a wrong-but-self-consistent denominator is invisible to every internal coherence/plausibility check). And NEVER dress a subset/plausibility bound as an independent reconciliation: when a remainder is unverifiable (untraded share classes absent from our data), the honest status is `plausible` (flag only the impossible), NOT `agrees` behind an arbitrary floor (a material founder class or a 49/51 split would falsely diverge; a wrong map at 55% would falsely agree). Self-review prompt: "does my 'independent' comparison value come from a genuinely different source, or the same primary data re-summed? And am I claiming agreement I can't actually verify?"
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/ownership_rollup.py::_classify_cross_check` + `_cross_validate_denominator` (#1647 pt5, `independent_concept` vs `per_class_subset_bound`); data-engineer skill I19; metrics-analyst skill §"Cross-source / validation contract"; spec `docs/specs/etl/2026-06-17-ownership-denominator-cross-check.md`.

### Market cap of a multi-class issuer is the total company, identical across siblings — detect dual-class from the curated table, not the noisy sibling count

- First seen in: PR (#1662, per-class market cap). The issue premise said the GOOGL `market_cap` should be the per-class figure "ClassA shares × price (~$2.16T), ~2× overstated today". Web + a 3-lens committee (metrics / valuation / data-engineer) falsified it: "market cap" is by universal convention the TOTAL company capitalization = Σ over classes (class_shares × class_price), shown IDENTICALLY on GOOG and GOOGL (companiesmarketcap: Alphabet one $4.424T total for both tickers). The per-class **float value** is a *different* metric that is never labelled "market cap". The legacy `combined_count × this-class-price` was the actual bug — it priced every class at one class's price and produced two different "caps" for one company (dev: GOOGL $4.474T vs GOOG $4.340T). Fixed = Σ class×price + untraded residual imputed at the largest traded class price → $4.4476T identical.
- Symptom (two traps): (1) accepting "per-class, 2×" at face value would have shipped a per-class float labelled "market cap" — half the company's value, contradicting every external source. (2) The first instinct for "is this a dual-class issuer?" — count shared-CIK tradable siblings — is NOISE: on dev 56 CIKs / 122 instruments have ≥2 co-CIK siblings, but only 3 are genuine curated dual-class equity; the rest are `.US` dual-listings, ETF trust families (BOIL/KOLD/SVXY…), warrants and preferreds (the exact junk the #1646 detector filters with CUSIP gates). A sibling-count oracle would have suppressed/mis-handled market cap for ~120 unrelated instruments.
- Prevention: for a metric with a settled industry definition, RESEARCH the definition (it has ONE truthful representation) and implement it — do not adopt a prompt's framing of what a field "should" show when it conflicts with convention. Market cap = total company, identical across share-class siblings; per-class float is a separate, separately-labelled stat. Detect "is this multi-class?" from a CURATED, CUSIP-disambiguated signal (here: presence in `instrument_class_shares_outstanding`, #1623's curated set), never a raw shared-CIK sibling count.
