- First seen in: #1982 (2026-07-13, Codex ckpt-2 #1, tender/going-private parser). `map_ciks_to_instruments` (`app/services/tender_offers.py`) resolved each SGML-header party CIK to an instrument via `SELECT DISTINCT ON (identifier_value) ... ORDER BY is_primary DESC` — one instrument per CIK, the primary. But one SEC CIK backs MULTIPLE in-universe instruments: share-class siblings (BRK.A/BRK.B, GOOG/GOOGL, ADR+ordinary) file under a single CIK yet are distinct `instruments` rows, each with its own `filing_events` row via the master-index path. Full-population dev scan: **69 CIKs map to >1 instrument; 33 tender accessions touch such a CIK.** The `DISTINCT ON` collapse wrote the tender event to only the primary class → the sibling class's Filings feed showed NO tender object despite its own `filing_events` row existing (the API join is on `(accession, instrument_id)`). Confirmed on real data: accession `0000950103-23-016099`, offeror CIK `0001978867` → instruments `[1050389, 15715]`, only `1050389` stored.
- Symptom: a resolver maps an external identifier (CIK, LEI, filer id) that is one-to-MANY against `instruments` using `DISTINCT ON (identifier)` / `LIMIT 1` / `is_primary` preference, so a fact that applies to the whole entity (a filing, an ownership event, a corporate action) lands on only ONE of the entity's listed instruments. Green wherever the entity has a single instrument (the common case); silently drops the fact from every sibling class. The lost figure is an absence on a secondary instrument's feed — invisible to a primary-instrument smoke check.
- Prevention: when the fact applies to the ENTITY (every instrument the CIK backs), the resolver returns `dict[cik, list[instrument_id]]` and the caller emits one row per instrument — never `DISTINCT ON (cik)`. Reserve primary-only resolution for facts that are genuinely per-listing (a quote, a per-instrument metric). Before writing a CIK→instrument resolver, ask "does this fact belong to the company or to the specific listing?" and verify the one-to-many surface on the full population: `SELECT identifier_value, count(*) FROM external_identifiers WHERE provider='sec' AND identifier_type='cik' GROUP BY 1 HAVING count(*)>1`. Distinct from the identity-keyed-collapse entry above (that merges distinct rows AT a chokepoint); this DROPS sibling targets AT resolution.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (CIK→instrument one-to-many FAQ); `app/services/tender_offers.py::map_ciks_to_instruments` (returns `dict[str, list[int]]` + why-comment); `tests/test_manifest_parser_sec_tender.py::test_share_class_sibling_cik_yields_row_per_instrument`.

### A dict-row key added to a DB-row read must land with its SELECT projection in the SAME diff — and an integration test must drive the REAL query

- First seen in: #2021 (2026-07-15, PR #2037 bot WARNING). `compute_band_for_instrument` (`app/services/fair_value_band.py`) gained six `trow["..."]` lookups (`operating_income_ttm`, `depreciation_amort_ttm`, `long_term_debt`, `short_term_debt`, `cash`, `interest_expense_ttm`) for the EV/EBITDA leg. The projection WAS added to `_TARGET_SQL` in the same commit and the db-tier test (`test_fair_value_band_io.py::test_single_name_run_anchors_to_materialized_cohort` drives `refresh_fair_value_band_batch` → the real `_TARGET_SQL`) would have KeyError'd otherwise — but the reviewer could not see the projection hunk adjacent to the lookups, and in the general case nothing structurally forces the two edits to travel together.
- Symptom: a service reads `row["new_col"]` under `psycopg.rows.dict_row`, but the SELECT feeding it was not extended (or was extended in a different commit/branch that didn't merge). Unit tests with hand-built dicts stay green; the real path raises `KeyError` for EVERY row at runtime — a whole-surface outage from a one-line omission. Same family as the #1955 sibling-model gap (field wired into one model, not its sibling): the contract spans two files with no compiler between them.
- Prevention: (1) when adding a dict-row key read, extend the SELECT in the SAME edit and cite the query constant in the commit; (2) at least one db-tier test must execute the REAL query into the REAL reader (not a mocked/hand-built row dict) so a projection gap fails in CI, not production; (3) self-review prompt: for every new `row["..."]` / `trow["..."]` key in the diff, name the query that projects it and point to its `+` hunk.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/test_fair_value_band_io.py` (real-query drive of `_TARGET_SQL`); `.claude/skills/engineering/pre-flight-review.md` §B same-class scan (row-access checks).

### Raw-store absence of an XBRL concept is NOT evidence the issuer doesn't tag it — the ingest allowlist gates what lands

- First seen in: #2036 (2026-07-15, spec full-pop verification). The issue premise said the concept-map class of D&A-null names tags `Depreciation` / `DepreciationAmortizationAndAccretionNet` — but a dev scan of `financial_facts_raw` found NEITHER concept for ANY instrument, which looked like a falsification. The real falsification ran the other way: `_extract_facts_from_section`'s docstring claimed "emit all by default" (#451 Phase A intent), but EVERY production caller passes `allowed_tags=_ALL_TRACKED_TAGS`, so raw holds only the ~78 tracked concepts. Absence of `Depreciation` in raw meant "we never stored it", not "issuers don't tag it" — fetching companyfacts for ALL 183 affected names showed 156 tag `Depreciation`.
- Symptom: a coverage/gap analysis quantifies issuer tagging behaviour by querying the landing table of an allowlist-gated ingest. Every un-allowlisted concept reads as "absent at the source", so the analysis silently inherits the allowlist as a fact about the world. Compounded by a stale docstring asserting the gate doesn't exist.
- Prevention: before claiming "issuer X doesn't tag concept Y" from a landing table, verify the ingest path stores Y at all (grep the writer's allowlist/filter — for fundamentals: `_ALL_TRACKED_TAGS` in `sec_fundamentals.py`); when the concept is un-tracked, go to the SOURCE (companyfacts JSON / frames API) for the population being analysed. Keep filter docstrings honest: a "default emit-all" claim is void when no caller uses the default.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_fundamentals.py::_extract_facts_from_section` docstring (corrected, #2036); `docs/proposals/etl/2026-07-15-fundamentals-dna-ytd-decumulation.md` §2.2.

### When a design's prose label and its validated harness disagree, freeze the HARNESS semantics — and the acceptance gate must compare like-with-like populations
- First seen in: #2032 (2026-07-15, fvb_v4 companion peer screen). The issue GO comment labelled the screen walk "D1 level-outer" but described one loop order in prose (level outer, widths inner) while the validated sim harness — the code every calibration number, hold-rate prediction, and acceptance threshold derived from — implemented the other (width-major: each width walks the full SIC ladder). The orders pick different cohorts on 62/152 ps and 29/113 ev held legs. Implementing the prose would have made the acceptance gate compare two different algorithms and spuriously "falsify" the sim. Second instance in the same gate: the sim's fallback-rate predictions were computed over PEER-BACKED legs only, while the naive post-ship census counted all screen-keyed legs (including peer-absent own-only legs) — 58% vs the predicted 83.5% held, a phantom gate failure until the populations were aligned (then exact: 83.5/94.4/89.0).
- Symptom: an acceptance check "fails" (or a design silently changes behaviour) not because the shipped code is wrong but because the shipped semantics or the measured population differs from what produced the predictions. Labels in issue prose are lossy; the harness code is the ground truth of what was validated.
- Prevention: (1) before implementing from a research writeup, DIFF the prose algorithm against the harness code that produced the numbers; on mismatch, freeze the harness semantics and document the discrepancy in the spec (full-pop compare the orders if the choice is load-bearing — #2032 spec §4.1 did, and width-major also won the tail-compression metric). (2) Every acceptance-gate prediction must name its POPULATION (peer-backed legs, ok bands, members) and the post-ship measurement must be computed over the same one before reading pass/fail.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `docs/proposals/valuation/2026-07-15-fvb-v4-companion-screen.md` §4.1 (walk-order decision block) + §7 gate 2 (dual-population note); `.claude/skills/valuation-analyst/SKILL.md` (fvb_v4 paragraph).

### A predicate's INPUT COLUMNS must be trust-verified for the affected population — a correct concept keyed on a correlate inherits that correlate's data weakness
- First seen in: #2043 (2026-07-15, fvb_v5 spec full-pop verification). The earnings-representativeness gate's concept (Damodaran ch.22: abnormal = deviant vs own history) was right, but the first two formulations were falsified by their INPUT columns, not their logic: (1) margin > 100% ("NI>revenue = non-operating") flagged 10 banks/REITs whose `financial_periods.revenue` is under-captured (UDR stored quarterly revenue $2.5M vs real ~$420M; TRS has a negative revenue quarter) — the predicate keyed a method change off a known-broken column; (2) margin-vs-own-history inherited the same weakness (REITs VNO/IVT/AHR/UDR false-fired "spiked"). The fix was to test the ACTUAL denominator's column (FY net income — no revenue involved), with the margin term demoted to a conjunction that can only SUPPRESS a fire (the artifact direction then cannot cause one). Same session, same class: EPS-history was rejected because XBRL FY EPS is as-reported (not split-adjusted) — another input-column property, not a logic property.
- Symptom: a full-pop census of a new predicate shows a cluster of false positives concentrated in one issuer class (banks, REITs, brokers, distributors). The logic reads fine; the flagged names share a data-capture weakness in one input column. Spot-check the raw stored values for 2-3 flagged names against reality before believing either the predicate or the census.
- Prevention: (1) for every column a new predicate/gate reads, ask "is this column trustworthy for the population this predicate will fire on?" — grep the prevention log + data-engineer skill for known capture weaknesses (financial-sector revenue, split-unadjusted EPS) and eyeball raw rows for a few flagged names; (2) prefer the column that IS the concept (the leg's actual denominator) over a correlate of it; (3) when a weak column is still wanted as a signal, structure it so the artifact direction cannot cause a fire (conjunction that only suppresses, never triggers).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `docs/proposals/valuation/2026-07-15-fvb-v5-earnings-representativeness-gate.md` §3 (falsification trail) + §5; `app/services/fair_value_band.py::earnings_nonrepresentative` docstring.

### Parallel sessions holding overlapping handoffs must stake on-issue and re-read comments immediately before posting — a session-start check is not enough
- First seen in: #2010 (2026-07-16 evening). Two live sessions (d73636e6, 15a95ca9) held overlapping relay handoffs with CONTRADICTORY fail branches ("gate FAIL → iterate + re-gate" vs "gate FAIL → operator-gate"). d73636e6 staked #2010 on-issue at 20:33 UTC and posted consolidated round-1 evidence at 21:38; 15a95ca9 posted a near-identical consolidated comment 62 SECONDS later — a duplicate, despite the stake existing for over an hour, because its freshness check happened at session start, not immediately before posting. Earlier instance of the class: #1995 close-out (07-11/12) — a session nearly double-posted the sweep verdict, saved only by re-checking `gh issue view` state first.
- Symptom: duplicate evidence/verdict comments minutes apart on the same issue; two sessions independently launching the same re-gate/benchmark chain; contradictory follow-up actions from divergent fail-branch instructions.
- Prevention: (1) stake ownership with a one-line issue comment BEFORE coding any handoff ticket; treat an existing fresh stake as "taken — pick something else"; (2) re-read the issue's latest comments IMMEDIATELY before posting any long-lived comment (evidence, verdict, close-out) — the race window is seconds, so a session-start check proves nothing; (3) before any close-out side effect (comment/close/merge/re-detach), re-check live state via gh/git; (4) operator-side: never hand two sessions the same ticket with different fail-branch instructions — the branches WILL diverge.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/CLAUDE.md` "Parallel-session coordination" section (#2075).

### Read the FULL review comment body before declaring a round triaged — a truncated read ships partial resolutions

- First seen in: PR #2090 (2026-07-17, #2067 judge hardening). The round-2 review carried FIVE findings (2× def14a merge-ref, 1 scheduler WARNING, 1 backfill NITPICK, 1 PREVENTION). Triage read the comment via `grep -A 12 "Claude Code Review"`, which truncated after the second finding — the PR merged with only 2/5 resolved. The PREVENTION comment (which contractually cannot be silently acknowledged) and two others sat unresolved until the operator flagged "lots of comments back"; the remainder shipped post-merge as PR #2091.
- Symptom: a review round "looks" resolved but the resolution comment addresses fewer findings than the review posted; PREVENTION/WARNING items silently drop; discovered only when a human re-reads the thread.
- Prevention: (1) read review comments with an untruncated fetch — `gh pr view N --comments` piped to a full read, or `gh api` for the specific comment body — never a fixed `-A N` grep window; (2) before posting a resolution comment, COUNT the findings in the review (`### [` headings) and assert the resolution set has the same count; (3) a REQUEST CHANGES verdict with any unread tail is an automatic stop — re-fetch the whole body first.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/review-resolution.md` (finding-count check).

### A parser/schema VERSION assertion in a db-tier test must reference the live constant — literal pins go silently stale because the db tier is off the push gate

- First seen in: #2100 (2026-07-22, fresh-agent pre-push review on the def14a-v6 bump). `tests/test_manifest_parser_def14a.py` asserted the literal `"def14a-v5"` (correctly bumped at v3→v4→v5 but missed at v5→v6 — a live regression of the diff), and `tests/test_def14a_ingest.py` asserted `"def14a-v3"` — stale across THREE bumps and failing the whole time, invisible because both are db-tier tests (`ebull_test_conn`) and the db tier is deliberately off the fast per-push gate (operator decision 2026-06-07). Every parser-version bump repeats this trap for every literal-pinned assertion.
- Symptom: `pytest -m db` fails on version-equality assertions that have nothing to do with the change being tested; or (worse) nobody runs the db tier for months and the stale assertion only surfaces mid-unrelated-work. The assertion's git history shows it being hand-bumped on some version changes and missed on others.
- Prevention: (1) a test asserting a version stamp reaches storage should import and compare the LIVE constant (`_PARSER_VERSION_DEF14A`), not a string literal — the invariant under test is "the constant is stamped through", not "the constant equals v5"; (2) when bumping any `*_VERSION` constant, grep the OLD value across `tests/` before committing (`grep -rn "def14a-v5" tests/`) — any hit is either a stale pin to fix or a deliberate history comment; (3) deliberate bump-visibility pins (wanting a test to FORCE acknowledgement of a bump) belong in the fast tier where they actually fire on push, not the db tier.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/test_manifest_parser_def14a.py` + `tests/test_def14a_ingest.py` (live-constant assertions with why-comments, #2100 PR #2103).

### A per-instrument-scoped rewash/backfill writer must mirror the live-ingest sibling fan-out — scoping the write to ONE instrument silently starves its share-class siblings

- First seen in: #2105 (2026-07-22, def14a comp rewash): the exec-comp rewash re-parsed under the requesting instrument only, while the live DEF 14A ingest fans issuer-scoped writes across every `siblings_for_issuer_cik` instrument — share-class siblings (METCB vs METC, GOOG vs GOOGL) kept STALE comp rows until PR #2106 re-fanned all 89 affected accessions. #828 PR-1 generalises the routing side: a Form 3/4/5 discovered via the reporting OWNER's EDGAR stream must bind to the parsed ISSUER's sibling set, and the fan-out lives inside the apply chokepoint so every caller (live drain, rewash, legacy pull) inherits it.
- Symptom: after a rewash/backfill "fixed" an instrument, its share-class sibling still renders the old figure; per-instrument endpoints disagree between siblings sharing one CIK; or (the #828 flavour) an owner instrument's page lists the issuer's filings.
- Prevention: (1) a rewash/backfill that re-runs a parser must call the SAME apply chokepoint the live path uses (`upsert_filing`, `upsert_form_3_filing`, `_record_def14a_observations_for_filing` — the sibling fan-out is inside), never a slimmed per-instrument variant; (2) a new repair script must grep the live parser's `siblings_for_issuer_cik` usage and mirror it; (3) post-repair verification covers EVERY sibling of each touched CIK, not just the requested symbol.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/insider_transactions.py::upsert_filing` + `app/services/insider_form3_ingest.py::upsert_form_3_filing` (#828 PR-1 `resolve_insider_writer_routing` — routing + fan-out at the chokepoint); `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` §2.10 note.

### A pure leaf util must not live inside a package whose __init__ has import side effects — importing pkg.leaf executes the whole __init__ and manufactures cycles

- First seen in: #2110 (2026-07-22). `app/services/manifest_parsers/_classify.py` (72-line pure discriminator, imports only psycopg) lived inside the parser-registry package whose `__init__` side-effect-imports every parser submodule. Python executes the package `__init__` before any `pkg.submodule` import, so a fresh process whose FIRST import was `insider_transactions` (which imported `manifest_parsers._classify`) fired the full registry init → `insider_345` → back into the still-partially-initialised insider module → `ImportError: partially initialized module`. THREE call sites grew load-bearing side-effect pre-imports as workarounds (scripts/rewash.py isort-pinned block, scripts/backfill_828_mislink_repair.py — 14/758 first-run failures — and instrument_analytics); the trap re-fired per fresh one-shot entrypoint.
- Symptom: a one-shot CLI or standalone entrypoint crashes on first import (or first lazy apply_fn call) with "partially initialized module", while app/pytest entrypoints work — they import the package first by unrelated side effect. Workaround comments saying "import order is LOAD-BEARING" accumulate.
- Prevention: (1) shared pure helpers live in plain modules (`app/services/upsert_classify.py`), never inside a side-effect package — importing a leaf must not boot a registry; (2) when a second call site needs an import-order workaround, that is the trigger to break the cycle structurally, not to copy the workaround; (3) fresh-process first-import regression guard: `tests/test_import_order_regression.py` subprocess-imports each formerly-cycling module as the interpreter's first import.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `tests/test_import_order_regression.py`; the #2110 move of `_classify` → `app/services/upsert_classify.py`.

### Ownership-XML parsers must tolerate the .txt full-submission SGML shape — index-derived URLs serve it, and stored-body reuse pins whatever was fetched first

- First seen in: #2110 (2026-07-22 full-pop scan). 14,279 `form3_xml`/`form4_xml`/`form5_xml` raw rows stored the `<SEC-DOCUMENT>` SGML full submission (master/daily-index discovery only knows the `.txt` URL; a legacy owner-stream fetch used it), and `parse_form_*_xml` rejected the wrapper → 11,331 filings tombstoned as "unparseable" with the real XML sitting embedded in the stored payload. The #1591 stored-body reuse then PINNED the bad bodies: the manifest parser short-circuits its proper primary-doc fetch whenever a raw row exists, so the cohort could never self-heal. The ticket premise said "13 rows" — the full-pop scan found 14,279 (sample-vs-population, again).
- Symptom: insider filings tombstoned despite a present raw body; `filing_raw_documents.source_url` ends `.txt`; payload starts `<SEC-DOCUMENT>`; wrapped def14a/pre14a bodies parse fine (HTML parsers tolerate the prefix) while XML parsers reject.
- Prevention: (1) parsers of EDGAR primary documents must unwrap the full-submission SGML container at the PARSER chokepoint (`insider_transactions._unwrap_sgml_submission` — first-close slice) so live ingest, stored-body reuse, and rewash all inherit it; (2) do NOT mutate stored payloads to "fix" them — the raw store keeps fetched bytes verbatim, the read side adapts; (3) any stored-body-reuse optimisation (#1591 pattern) makes the parser the ONLY self-heal path — a fetch-side fix alone can never repair pinned rows.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/insider_transactions.py::_unwrap_sgml_submission` (+ SGML cases in `tests/test_insider_form3_parser.py` / `tests/test_insider_transactions.py`); `scripts/backfill_2110_sgml_tombstones.py` (cohort re-apply); `.claude/skills/data-sources/sec-edgar.md` §3.3 note.

## Augment writers sharing a primary writer's transaction must be savepoint-isolated at EVERY call site

- Symptom: an augment (drift detector, exec-comp, any best-effort enrichment) is wrapped in a savepoint-isolated best-effort helper at its per-filing call sites, but a NEW call site (a batch/weekly job) calls the underlying function bare on a connection whose transaction also holds the primary sync's writes — one augment exception then rolls back the whole primary batch, converting a nice-to-have failure into loss of the job's actual work.
- Prevention: the best-effort contract is a property of the CALL SITE, not the helper — every locus that runs an augment on a shared-transaction connection wraps it in `try: with conn.transaction(): ...` (savepoint under the open tx, #1700) + `except Exception: logger.exception(...)`. Self-review grep before push: bare calls of the augment entrypoint (e.g. `detect_drift(`) not inside a try/savepoint.
- First seen in: #966 PR #2120 review round 3 (WARNING) — `detect_drift(conn)` bare in `ownership_observations_backfill` while the three per-filing loci used `run_drift_detection_best_effort`.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/workers/scheduler.py` (weekly drift repair, savepoint-isolated).

## DISTINCT ON / ROW_NUMBER without a UNIQUE final tie-break diverges between single-key and bulk plans

- Symptom: a per-row reader (`_read_latest_two_fy_facts`, `DISTINCT ON (concept, fiscal_year)`) whose `ORDER BY` tie-breakers (`period_end DESC, filed_date DESC, accession_number DESC`) are not jointly UNIQUE looked stable in production, but its bulk twin (`WHERE instrument_id = ANY(...)`, same `ORDER BY` + `PARTITION BY`) picked a DIFFERENT winner on tied rows. The single-key query has a consistent plan → consistent (arbitrary) physical order → stable-looking pick; the bulk query has a different plan → different order → different arbitrary pick. `financial_facts_raw` co-tags an annual value (`period_start`≈`period_end`−1yr) and a Q4 stub (91-day span) both `fiscal_period='FY'` at the same `period_end`/filing → 495 instruments had ≥1 such tie, and the golden silently picked the Q4 stub for some (evidence-only Piotroski/Altman, so it hid). A Phase-1 A/B that computed analytics ONCE and shared it never exercised the divergence.
- Prevention: any `DISTINCT ON` / `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY ...)` / `fetchone() ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` whose leading keys aren't provably unique MUST end its `ORDER BY` with the surrogate PK (`fact_id DESC`, `id DESC`) — on BOTH the per-row and the bulk form, so they agree AND are reproducible run-to-run. Prefer a domain tie-break first where one exists (here `(period_end - period_start) DESC` picks the annual row an annual model needs), then the PK as the unique backstop. When bulking a reader for a batch job, prove equivalence on the FULL population via a same-process A/B — the tie only bites rows that have duplicates, so a sample misses it. Also documented in `.claude/skills/engineering/sql-correctness.md` (§ "DISTINCT ON / ROW_NUMBER need a UNIQUE final tie-break").
- First seen in: #2127 Phase 2 (PR bulk-analytics) — full-pop A/B surfaced piotroski/altman divergence on 495 instruments; fixed by adding `(period_end - period_start) DESC NULLS LAST, fact_id DESC` to both `_read_latest_two_fy_facts` and `_bulk_read_latest_two_fy_facts`.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/sql-correctness.md`; `app/services/instrument_analytics.py` (both FY readers share the tie-break).

## A lint gate's file-glob is part of its contract — a `.tsx`-only walk leaves class strings in `.ts` modules structurally unguarded

- Symptom: a hygiene gate reports "no violations" while the exact class of defect it exists to catch sits in the tree. Here `frontend/scripts/check-dark-classes.mjs` walked only `.tsx`, so `components/instrument/eightKSeverity.ts` — a shared severity→Tailwind tone map feeding the 8-K chips on `EightKDetailPanel` + `EightKListPage` — carried light-only tinted backgrounds with no dark-mode partners, i.e. precisely the #970 washed-out-in-dark bug the gate's Check F was written for. The gate had been green over it for its whole life. Extracting a tone map from a component into a plain `.ts` module is a NORMAL refactor, and it silently moves the code out of the gate's field of view.
- Prevention: (1) when a gate scans "the frontend", its walk covers `.ts` AND `.tsx` — extension filters must match where the pattern can actually appear, not where it happened to appear first; (2) when extracting constants out of a component into a sibling module, re-run the gates and confirm the file COUNT rose (262 → 379 here) — a silent count is the tell that the extraction escaped a check; (3) these gates are textual and line-based, so a doc comment quoting a Tailwind class trips them — describe classes in prose inside comments.
- First seen in: #1908 PR-2 (2026-07-24), while consolidating pills into one `Badge`.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/scripts/check-dark-classes.mjs` (walk covers `.ts`); `.claude/skills/frontend/design-system.md` (Badge section — tones are semantic values, colour classes live only in `Badge`).

## A chart that binds the theme AND imports the raw palette is light-only, and no gate sees it

- Symptom: `frontend/src/components/fundamentals/fundamentalsCharts.tsx` called `useChartTheme()` and bound it to `theme`, then drew its series with `lightTheme.accent[…]` — **23 raw-palette reads against 14 theme reads**, on a page that ships dark mode. Nothing flagged it: `pnpm dark:check` keys off Tailwind bg/border/hover **class pairs**, and these were inline hex values passed as recharts props from a JS module, so all six of its checks are structurally blind to them. It also looked fine on screen, because `darkTheme` currently re-exports `lightTheme`'s accents — the defect was latent, and would have surfaced as a light-palette page the first time an accent diverged. The presence of a correct `useChartTheme()` binding in the same file is what makes this survive review: the file reads as theme-aware.
- Prevention: (1) importing `lightTheme` / `darkTheme` **by name** anywhere outside `lib/chartTheme.ts` is the smell — `useChartTheme()` is the only read, and a component that has both is a bug in progress; (2) do not treat "renders correctly in dark mode" as evidence when the two palettes share values — grep the ratio of raw-palette to theme reads instead; (3) a gate blind to a defect class must be *extended or joined*, not trusted: the shape here (inline style values, not class strings) needed a new textual gate, `frontend/scripts/check-chart-integrity.mjs`. Related: the same file drew discrete quarterly financials with recharts' `monotone` curve and `dot={false}`, so the rendered line passed through values no issuer reported — a *correctness* defect wearing a styling prop's clothing.
- First seen in: #2185 (2026-07-31), fundamentals drill-page chart integrity.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/scripts/check-chart-integrity.mjs` (bans the raw palettes outside `chartTheme.ts` + the cubic curve type, scoped to `components/` + `pages/` since #2190; `lib/chartTheme.ts` is out of scope by path, not by exemption); `frontend/package.json` (`charts:check`); `.githooks/pre-push`; `.claude/skills/frontend/design-system.md` (Chart theming + "Chart series must not draw unreported values").

## A gate introduced at narrow scope leaves the same defect live everywhere else, and nothing schedules the widening

- Symptom: #2185 fixed cubic interpolation and hardcoded light-palette reads on the fundamentals drill page and shipped `charts:check` to hold the line — correctly scoped to `components/fundamentals/`, because a gate that is red on day one over pre-existing debt gets disabled rather than obeyed. What that scoping bought was measured a day later (#2190): **27 live violations across 10 files** — 11 cubic-curve series and 16 raw-palette reads — including `components/reports/PerformanceChart.tsx`, an operator-facing statement chart on the very surface #2178 had worked earlier in the same session. The defect class was named, understood, gated, and still live on 8 of 9 chart surfaces. The narrow scope was the right call; the absence of a *follow-through ticket filed at the same time* was not.
- Prevention: when a gate ships scoped below the blast radius of the defect it names, the same PR (a) measures and records the count outside the scope, and (b) files the widening ticket. Widen with a **counted ratchet**, not a skip-list: per file and per rule, matched EXACTLY. `actual > cap` catches regression; `actual < cap` catches a burn-down that forgot to lower the number — without that second direction every fix silently leaves headroom and the "temporary" mechanism becomes permanent. Unlisted files fail on their first violation, so the durable guarantee ("a new chart cannot regress") lands in one PR instead of waiting on every fix. This is NOT the skip-list #987 drained: a skip-list is binary and lets debt grow invisibly inside a listed file; a counted ratchet cannot. Make the gate refuse to run on an empty ratchet so the mechanism cannot outlive the debt.
- Related trap: these gates are line-based, so a doc comment *quoting* the banned literal trips them with no violation to fix — it can never be burned down and would sit in the ratchet forever. `Sparkline.tsx` recommended the now-banned raw-palette call in its JSDoc; the fix is prose (prevention log → #1908 PR-2), not a ratchet entry.
- First seen in: #2190 (2026-08-02), widening #2185's gate.
- Outcome: the ratchet was drained to zero across #2190 + #2197 (10 files, 9 PRs) and the mechanism deleted. The refuse-to-run-on-empty guard did its job — it forced the final fix and the teardown into the SAME commit, because leaving the entry failed the stale-cap check while deleting it tripped the empty guard. Neither half was mergeable alone, so the mechanism provably could not outlive the debt. Keep that guard in any future ratchet.
- Follow-on trap measured during the drain: the counts recorded offending **lines**, not offending references — the scanner pushes at most one violation per banned name per line. The identical defect (a `<Cell>` fill ternary reading the raw palette on two branches) was capped at 2 in `InsiderByOfficer.tsx` and 1 in `InsiderNetByMonth.tsx` purely because the formatter broke one across lines. Fine for a ratchet, which only compares a file against itself, but the headline count understates the real work and a cap must never be read as a reference count.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/scripts/check-chart-integrity.mjs` (now a plain tree-wide check, ratchet removed); `.claude/skills/frontend/design-system.md` (Chart theming — ONE source).

## Moving a colour read onto the theme is only half the fix for a canvas charting library

- Symptom: #2197's last burn-down replaced module-scope light-palette constants in `ChartWorkspaceCanvas.tsx` with `theme.indicator[...]` / `theme.compare[...]`, and moved `RichTooltip` onto `useChartTheme()`. Codex flagged the resulting shape before push: lightweight-charts series colours are passed to `chart.addSeries(...)` at **creation**, and the effects that create indicator/compare series do not rerun on a theme change. `RichTooltip` reads the resolved theme at render and does. So after a theme toggle a drawn indicator line would keep its creation-time colour while its own tooltip readout moved — **the line and its readout disagreeing about the colour of the same series**. Before the change both read the same frozen light table and agreed (both wrong together), so the "fix" strictly introduced a new inconsistency in the very class it was removing.
- Prevention: when moving a colour onto `useChartTheme()`, ask where the value is *applied*, not just where it is read. Recharts re-renders on prop change so a read is sufficient; **lightweight-charts applies options imperatively at creation**, so every live series needs recolouring from the `[theme]` effect too (`ChartWorkspaceCanvas` recolours `indicatorRefs` and `compareLineRefs` there). Tell: a component where one consumer of a colour is JSX and another is an `addSeries` call. Corollary — keep the colour selection in a shared pure helper (`compareColorAt(theme, idx)`) so the creation path and the recolour path cannot drift on an index formula. Related: adding `applyOptions` to a series is new charting-lib API surface for that component's `vi.mock`, so run the FULL `pnpm --dir frontend test`, not `test:unit` (prevention log → #1841).
- Corollary on "the palettes are identical anyway": that rationale is what put these reads at module scope in the first place, and module scope is exactly where the hook cannot run — the shortcut is self-reinforcing. It is also false in general: `primaryLine` already diverges (`#1e293b` light / `#f1f5f9` dark), visibly flipping the compare-mode primary line black↔white on toggle, while only the *saturated* slots alias.
- First seen in: #2197 / PR #2206 (2026-08-02), Codex pre-push review.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/src/pages/components/ChartWorkspaceCanvas.tsx` (theme effect recolours live series; `compareColorAt` shared); `.claude/skills/frontend/design-system.md` (Chart theming — ONE source).

## A chart whose two series are equal by an accounting identity cannot carry signal

- Symptom: the L2 fundamentals page shipped an "Assets vs Liabilities + Equity" stacked-bar snapshot. Those two quantities are equal **by the accounting identity** (`A = L + E`), so the chart was guaranteed to render two bars of the same length for every instrument in every period — a pane that costs a fetch, a render and a scroll position while being incapable of varying. It survived because each half is individually a real, correctly-sourced metric; only the *comparison* is vacuous.
- Prevention: when specifying a chart, state what makes the plotted quantity **vary** before drawing it. If the answer is "it doesn't — the two sides are definitionally equal", the chart is a data-integrity display at best (`assets ≈ L + E` as a parse check), not an analytical one, and belongs in a validation view or nowhere. Replace it with a decomposition that has genuine variance — here, a net-debt trend (`COALESCE(long,0) + COALESCE(short,0) − cash`, the repo's own settled rule) answering "is the balance sheet getting safer?".
- First seen in: #2185 (2026-07-31), spec §1.6.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/src/lib/fundamentalsMetrics.ts::buildNetDebt`; `.claude/skills/metrics-analyst/SKILL.md` ("Total assets / liabilities / equity" — chart: none, do not re-add).

## Reusing a shared formatter at a new call site exercises value classes the old call sites never produced

- Symptom: `formatBigMoney` (`frontend/src/lib/format.ts`) concatenated `` `${sym}${formatBigNumber(n)}` ``. `formatBigNumber` emits its own leading `-`, so every negative rendered as **`US$-10.75B`** — the sign inside the denomination. It had shipped that way since #1978 and was invisible because its only callers were `OfferingBlock.tsx`, which passes always-positive offering proceeds. #2185 routed the fundamentals money axes through it, where net debt, investing/financing cash flow, negative FCF and negative operating income all cross zero (AAPL FY2012 net debt = −10,746,000,000), and the bug became the default panel's rendering. Its unit tests asserted only positive inputs, so the suite stayed green.
- Prevention: when pointing an existing helper at a new call site, enumerate the **value classes the new caller produces that the old ones could not** — sign, zero, null, magnitude bands, locale/currency — and add a test per class *before* claiming the reuse is a no-op. "Reuse > reinvent" makes the helper's untested branches your bug. Corollary for money specifically: the sign belongs outside the symbol, and a currency-aware formatter fed backend text (`reported_currency` is `TEXT NOT NULL` with no shape CHECK, `sql/032_financial_data_enrichment_p1.sql:121`) must not throw — `new Intl.NumberFormat(_, {style:"currency", currency})` raises `RangeError` on a non-ISO-4217 code, and inside a recharts `tickFormatter` that takes down the whole chart rather than one label.
- First seen in: #2185 (2026-07-31), adverse review of the chart-integrity branch.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/src/lib/format.ts` (`formatBigMoney` sign handling + `getFormatter` try/catch); `frontend/src/lib/format.test.ts` (negative and invalid-code cases).

## A frontend gate that re-derives a metric must cite the backend's settled rule, not re-invent it

- Symptom: `buildFcf` gated the FCF line on `operating_cf !== null && capex !== null`, while the backend's settled treatment is `operating_cf − ABS(COALESCE(capex, 0))` (`app/services/fcf_yield.py:111` quarterly, `:132` annual) — capex NULL → 0, OCF the only strict input. Full population (dev `financial_periods`, `superseded_at IS NULL`, per instrument): **1,142 FY / 1,247 Q1 instruments report OCF and never report capex**, so they saw the empty state for a series computable for every one of them; a further ~1,000 per basis were mixed, breaking the line at exactly the periods where the right-hand #671 TTM-yield line — which already COALESCEs capex — still plotted a value. One chart, two contradictory capex rules, and the empty-state copy asserted the wrong rule to the operator.
- Prevention: any frontend metric that has a backend/SQL counterpart must **cite the backend rule at the derivation site** and match its null-handling exactly. Diverging COALESCE-vs-strict-null semantics between a line and its own overlay is the tell. When a chart shows an empty state, verify what fraction of the population that empty state is claiming — measure per instrument on the full population, never a sample or the 5-name smoke panel.
- First seen in: #2185 (2026-07-31); the spec (§3.4) had already written the rule verbatim — *"Any implementation that gates the FCF line on `capex IS NOT NULL` is wrong"* — and the implementation shipped the forbidden gate anyway.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/src/lib/fundamentalsMetrics.ts::buildFcf` (docstring cites `fcf_yield.py:111,132` + the measured cost); `frontend/src/lib/fundamentalsMetrics.test.ts`; `frontend/src/components/fundamentals/fundamentalsCharts.test.tsx`.

## A bare `text-<color>` class has no dark-mode gate — only bg / border / hover pairs are checked

- Symptom: a component renders semantic colour through text alone (`text-emerald-700` / `text-red-700` with no `dark:` partner) and reads dim or low-contrast in dark mode, while `pnpm dark:check` stays green. The gate's six checks (A-F in `frontend/scripts/check-dark-classes.mjs`) all key off background / border / hover tokens; a text-only tone is outside every one of them. `RollingPnlStrip`'s private stat tile carried three such classes from #315 Phase 2 until #1908 PR-5 — the sibling `StatTile` one row above it had the correct light+dark pair the whole time, so the same semantic rendered two different colours on one screen.
- Prevention: (1) semantic colour carried by TEXT gets its `dark:` partner by hand — the gate will not remind you; (2) better, do not hand-roll the element at all: if a sibling component already renders this shape (`StatTile`, `Badge`), reuse it and inherit its theme pairs — a near-copy is how the two drifted apart; (3) when reviewing a diff that adds a semantic text colour, check for the partner explicitly, because CI will not.
- First seen in: #1908 PR-5 (2026-07-24), consolidating the dashboard stat rows.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/frontend/design-system.md` (Density — "Every stat tile is `StatTile`"); `frontend/src/components/dashboard/RollingPnlStrip.tsx` (now renders `StatTile`).

## An observations UPSERT keyed on a PARSED value makes a parser fix additive, not corrective — the re-wash cleans nothing

- Symptom: #2140 fixed the DEF 14A parser so 3,209 rows stopped persisting a share count as `holder_name` and 704 stopped splitting one person across two identities on an interior newline. Re-running the parser over the stored bodies would have changed **nothing** operator-visible. `record_def14a_observation` is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (instrument_id, holder_name_key, ownership_nature, period_end, source_document_id) DO UPDATE`, and `holder_name_key` is `lower(trim(holder_name))` — i.e. **the conflict key is derived from the very value the parser fix changes**. A corrected row therefore lands under a NEW key and the broken row stays live. Worse, the reconciler looks self-healing and isn't: `refresh_def14a_current`'s MERGE prunes with `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE THEN DELETE`, but its source set is `ownership_def14a_observations WHERE known_to IS NULL` — which still contains the broken rows, so they keep their `_current` row alive forever. The stale code comment at the call site asserted the opposite ("record_def14a_observation is itself UPSERT so re-ingest of the same accession (parser bump) refreshes existing rows in place") — true only while the parser keeps producing the same name.
- Prevention: before claiming a parser fix + re-wash will repair stored data, ask **"is the conflict key derived from a field this fix can change?"** If yes, the writer needs a filing-scoped **supersede**-then-reassert, not upsert-only. Put it inside the shared per-filing writer (not at one call site) so every locus — first ingest, re-wash, backfill — inherits it; on first ingest it is a no-op. Scope it to `(instrument_id, source_document_id)` and **not** the accession alone whenever the writer fans out over share-class siblings — an accession-wide sweep makes each sibling's write supersede the previous sibling's rows. Supersede via `known_to = NOW()` (invariant I6: never hard-delete observations) and pair it with `known_to = NULL` in the writer's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, so rows the new parse still emits are revived in the same transaction and only the rows it no longer emits stay tombstoned, audit history intact. #953 used a hard DELETE for the 13F equivalent solely because its writer did NOT clear `known_to` on conflict — that is a mechanical gap to close, not a reason to destroy observations.
- First seen in: #2140 (2026-07-24), DEF 14A beneficial-ownership parser quality. Same shape as #953 (13F `institutional_holdings` re-wash).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/def14a_ingest.py::_record_def14a_observations_for_filing` (filing-scoped tombstone + comment); `app/services/ownership_observations.py::record_def14a_observation` (`known_to = NULL` revive clause); `.claude/skills/data-engineer/SKILL.md` (I6).

## Ownership-specific cleaning added to a SHARED name helper silently rewrites the exec-comp path

- Symptom: #2140 needed two repairs on DEF 14A holder names (flatten interior newlines, strip an unbracketed trailing footnote digit) and put them in `_clean_holder_name` — which reads like the holder-name helper but is **shared with the Item 402(c) SCT path**. `_normalize_first_cell` feeds stacked title FRAGMENTS through it, so the new trailing-punctuation strip turned the fragment `'EVP,'` into `'EVP'` and HBNC's NEO title came out `'EVP Chief Financial Officer'` instead of `'EVP, Chief Financial Officer'`. Caught only because `test_hbnc_non_lexicon_second_row_fragment` existed; the spec had asserted "SCT output unchanged" on the reasoning that every SCT consumer already flattens `\n` — true of the newline change, false of the footnote change bundled with it.
- Prevention: a helper named for one domain is not owned by it — grep every caller before adding domain-specific behaviour, and when the callers span two parsers, add a **new** helper for the narrow path rather than widening the shared one (`_clean_beneficial_holder_name` here) and say so in the shared helper's docstring. When a change bundles two repairs, verify the blast-radius claim for **each** separately; "consumers already do X" can be true for one and false for the other. Pin the split with a test that asserts both behaviours side by side.
- First seen in: #2140 (2026-07-24).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_def14a.py::_clean_holder_name` (docstring warns off ownership-specific cleaning); `tests/test_sec_def14a_parser.py::TestHolderNameNewlineNormalisation::test_sct_name_title_split_is_unaffected`.

## `<> ALL(empty array)` is VACUOUSLY TRUE — an empty-set supersede deletes everything

- Symptom: a "delete the rows the new parse no longer emits" query written as `DELETE … WHERE holder_name <> ALL(%(names)s::text[])` is correct for every non-empty name list and catastrophically wrong for an empty one. Postgres evaluates `'x' <> ALL(ARRAY[]::text[])` to **TRUE** (verified directly), so when a re-parse legitimately or transiently yields ZERO rows, the predicate matches every row and the whole accession's typed data is deleted. #2140 introduced this while fixing the opposite defect (an upsert keyed on a parsed value leaving stale rows live) — so the corrective fix reintroduced the same class as a *destructive* one. Both call sites passed `[h.holder_name for h in parsed.rows]` unconditionally, and one of them had no `if parsed.rows:` guard even though the observations write-through eight lines below did.
- Prevention: any supersede/reconcile query using `<> ALL(...)`, `NOT IN (...)`, or `NOT EXISTS` against a caller-supplied set MUST short-circuit on the empty set — `if not names: return 0` — placed in the shared helper, not at each call site, so future callers inherit it. Add a regression test asserting existing rows SURVIVE a zero-item call; the happy-path test cannot catch this because it always passes a non-empty list. Same reasoning applies to `= ANY(empty)` (always FALSE, so a refresh silently becomes a no-op) — check which vacuous direction your predicate has before shipping it. Empirically verify the semantics (`SELECT 'x' <> ALL(ARRAY[]::text[])`) rather than reasoning about them.
- First seen in: #2140 (2026-07-25, review bot BLOCKING on PR #2159).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/def14a_ingest.py::_supersede_dropped_holdings` (empty-set guard + comment); `tests/test_def14a_ingest.py::test_zero_row_reparse_preserves_existing_typed_rows`.

## Promoting a header row must not strip the table's identity evidence from the SCORER

- Symptom: #2140 D2 taught `_parse_table_html` to promote a two-row header's row 1 to `column_headers` when row 0 is a spanning row. It deliberately did NOT fold row 1 into `score_headers` — so for every table whose row 0 is the *share-class* row Item 403's "any class" disclosure produces, the scorer never saw Item 403's own prescribed captions at all. `0000908311-26-000065` scored **0** on `('Common Stock', 'Series A Preferred Stock')` where its caption row scores 13; `0000004904-25-000043` scored 1 instead of 11. Both fell under the floor of 3. **181 accessions returned zero rows** and were held only by the rewash safety guard. The two symptom buckets looked unrelated — 58 fell below the floor outright, 123 still qualified but lost the sibling gate (`sc >= 6 or sc == window_best`) to a layout `<table>` holding a footnote paragraph or a "Voting Instructions" prose block that out-scored them on incidental keyword hits.
- Prevention: **column resolution and table identification read different rows — decide each separately, and never let a fix for one silently re-scope the other.** When a parser promotes, re-derives, or re-orders header cells, ask explicitly "does the SELECTOR still see the evidence that identifies this table?" and assert it with a test that pins the score, not only the extracted rows. The corollary bit both ways here: the original non-fold was justified by a real counter-example (an Item 402(f) label row lifting an award table into a tie), which a *later* fix in the same file — the `_ITEM_402_AWARD_MARKERS` disqualifier — had since inverted. **A documented "we deliberately did not do X" comment is evidence about the code as it stood, not a standing invariant; re-check its premise against everything that landed after it.**
- First seen in: #2158 (2026-07-25), fixing a defect introduced by #2140.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_def14a.py::_parse_table_html` (both arms fold); `tests/test_sec_def14a_parser.py::TestPromotedLabelRowStillScoresTheTable`.

## Feeding a scorer MORE text newly QUALIFIES tables — measure what clears the floor, not just what gets disqualified

- Symptom: the #2158 fold above hands `_score_table_headers` the promoted caption row as well as the parent. That makes a whole class of table **visible to the scorer for the first time**: the Reg S-K **Item 402(g)** "Option Exercises and Stock Vested" table renders its captions under a spanning `('Option Awards', 'Stock Awards')` row, so unfolded it scored **0** and folded it scores **5** — over the floor of 3, and carrying none of the existing `_ITEM_402_AWARD_MARKERS`. The first full-population promotion audit reported "0 shapes where the fold LOWERS the score, 0 newly disqualified" and looked clean; the hazard was entirely in the direction nobody had counted. On the guard-blocked cohort the fold alone "recovered" **5 accessions whose recovered rows were vesting data emitted as beneficial owners**.
- Prevention: when a change widens a scorer's input, the audit must count **shapes that newly clear the floor**, not only shapes that lose score or become disqualified — the failure mode of a *more informed* scorer is admitting things it previously could not see, and a disqualifier tuned against the old input has no coverage of them. Run it full-population before trusting the change: a 300-body smoke already showed 47 such shapes. And when a defect is created by the fix in the same PR, fix it in the same PR — do not ship the widening and file the fallout (#2140: 3 of 14 defects were self-inflicted by earlier fixes in that same PR).
- First seen in: #2158 (2026-07-25).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_def14a.py::_ITEM_402_AWARD_MARKERS` (Item 402(g) captions + why-comment); `scripts/ab_2140_def14a_parser.py --audit` ("shapes newly CLEARING the floor (hazard)"); `tests/test_sec_def14a_parser.py::TestItem402gOptionExercisesAndStockVested`.

## A verification harness that RECONSTRUCTS the control condition can silently report "no drift"

- Symptom: #2158's `--audit` mode runs both scoring modes in ONE process by reconstructing what `main`'s `score_headers` would have been — strip the promoted row back off, unless the promotion came from the legacy arm, which folded on `main` too. The arm test used only the legacy arm's KEYWORD condition and omitted its width condition (`len(parent_headers) < max_data_width`). Every label-arm promotion whose promoted row happens to contain a legacy keyword was therefore mislabelled as "legacy, no change" — and an Item 402(c) SCT label row *always* contains one, because § 229.402(c)(2)(x) prescribes a **Total** column. The audit reported **zero** SCT drift, which read as a clean blast-radius proof. The real main-vs-branch A/B, running two actual code paths, showed **75 accessions going from 0 SCT rows to 12-18 real ones**. Had the change been harmful rather than beneficial, the harness would have concealed it just as completely.
- Prevention: when a harness simulates the control arm instead of running it, it inherits every branch of the condition it is simulating — reproduce **all** of them, and prefer a real two-checkout A/B as the authority whenever the two disagree. Treat a **clean** result from a simulated control with more suspicion than a dirty one: a harness bug and a genuine no-op are indistinguishable at the output, so cross-check the clean claim against an independent measurement before citing it as a gate. Here the totals line (`sct_rows 66,445 -> 67,312`) contradicted the audit's zero and was the thread that unpicked it — always reconcile aggregate counters against per-item drift lists rather than reading them separately.
- First seen in: #2158 (2026-07-25), in the ticket's own verification tooling.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `scripts/ab_2140_def14a_parser.py::_audit` (`unfolded_headers` reproduces both arm conditions + why-comment).
## A prevention entry fixed on ONE arm of a function does not cover its siblings — audit every arm, and every write the live chokepoint makes

- Symptom: the entry above on rewash sibling fan-out ("a rewash/backfill that re-runs a parser must call the SAME apply chokepoint the live path uses — the sibling fan-out is inside") was written for #2105 and applied to the **Item 402(c) exec-comp** arm of `_rewash_def14a`. The **Item 403 beneficial-ownership arm of the same function**, twenty lines above it, was left on the slimmed per-instrument path — and its `DELETE FROM def14a_beneficial_holdings WHERE accession_number = %s` clears the accession for ALL siblings while the re-INSERT covers only the one instrument `LIMIT 1` happened to resolve. Full-population: **41 `(accession, instrument)` pairs / 31 instruments / 457 live observation rows** with their typed backing deleted and their observations frozen under the old parser's names, which `refresh_def14a_current` then keeps alive forever. Two further writes diverged for the same reason: the drift re-check ran single-instrument where the live path fans it, and the **ESOP write-through was absent from rewash entirely** — zero `esop` references in the whole module.
- Prevention: when a prevention entry is applied, grep the **enclosing function and its siblings** for the same shape rather than patching the call site that failed — the second occurrence is usually in the same file. And when the rule is "mirror the live chokepoint", diff the live path's body **call for call** and enumerate every write it makes (typed rows, observations, `_current` refresh, ESOP, drift, supersede); the ticket names the one write someone noticed, which is rarely all of them. Note that a repair loop keyed only on the *typed* table cannot reach rows an earlier buggy rewash already deleted — union the surviving **observations** in as well, or the backfill silently skips exactly the damage it exists to fix.
- First seen in: #2157 (2026-07-25); the un-covered arm of the #2105 entry, found by Codex ckpt-1.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/services/rewash_filings.py::_def14a_holdings_instrument_ids` (siblings ∪ typed ∪ live observations) + the per-instrument loop in `_rewash_def14a`; `tests/test_rewash_filings.py::test_def14a_rewash_fans_out_over_every_instrument_holding_the_accession`.

## A cell with two render paths must render the same currency on BOTH — fixing the fallback leaves the live path stamping the wrong symbol

- Symptom: #2129 gave every portfolio/dashboard money cell a per-row `currency` (display currency normally, the instrument's native currency when the FX rate was missing) and fixed `LivePriceCell`'s REST-fallback path to honour it. The cell's OTHER path — the live SSE tick — kept calling `liveTickDisplayPrice`, which prefers the tick's own `display` block. A tick carries BOTH a native triple and a converted display triple, so on an FX-degraded row the first tick flipped Price to the display currency while Invested / Value / P&L stayed native: a row that agreed with itself on first paint and disagreed a second later, with no state change the operator could see. #2129 recorded this as a known lower-priority edge rather than a miss, but the shape is the trap — a currency contract enforced on one of a component's paths reads as done.
- Prevention: when a component can source the same figure from more than one place (REST snapshot vs live stream, cache vs fetch, optimistic vs confirmed), the unit that owns the currency/unit contract is the COMPONENT, not the path — enumerate every `return` that renders the value and confirm each is denominated in the same declared field. Prefer resolving it as a **lookup, not a mode flag**: pass the one currency the surface is in and have the accessor pick the block matching it (`liveTickPriceIn(tick, currency)`), so no call site can forget a boolean. When NO available block is in the required currency, render nothing live and keep the correctly-labelled fallback — a stale-but-true number beats a live number wearing the wrong symbol. Self-review prompt: grep the component for every `formatMoney(` and check the currency argument is the same expression on every branch.
- First seen in: #2133 (2026-07-29), split out of #2129 by Codex ckpt-2.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `frontend/src/lib/useLiveQuote.ts::liveTickPriceIn`; `frontend/src/components/quotes/LivePriceCell.tsx`; `frontend/src/components/quotes/LivePriceCell.test.tsx`; `.claude/skills/frontend/operator-ui-conventions.md` §"Formatting helpers".

## `git reset --soft origin/main` to squash a WIP commit silently REVERTS whatever landed on main in the meantime

- Symptom: a feature branch's PR diff contains a clean revert of an unrelated, already-merged PR — its source change, its tests, its prevention-log entry — with nothing in the branch's own commit touching those files. Here #2148 was branched from `origin/main` at `73f721ef`, worked on, and staged behind a throwaway `wip:` commit. Between branching and the squash, #2133 merged and `origin/main` advanced to `756c6529` (a `git fetch` during that merge updated the local remote-tracking ref). The squash step — `git reset --soft origin/main && git commit` — then re-parented the work onto the NEW main while `--soft` deliberately preserved the OLD index and worktree. New base + old content is, by definition, a commit that undoes every change between the two. `git merge-base branch main` returning main's own HEAD is the tell: the branch claims to descend from a main it does not contain.
- Prevention: never pass a MOVING ref to `git reset --soft`. To squash a scratch commit, name the immutable commit you actually branched from — `git reset --soft <sha>` (record it when you branch), or `git reset --soft HEAD~1` for a single wip commit, or `git commit --amend`. If you do rebase onto a newer base, that is a REBASE (`git rebase origin/main`), which replays your changes on top of theirs; `reset --soft` replaces theirs with your stale tree. Self-review before pushing: run `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` and confirm every listed file is one this branch means to touch — a file you never opened appearing there is the whole signal. This is cheap and catches the class instantly.
- Note the review bot caught this as BLOCKING and the author's first instinct was to dismiss it as a two-dot-vs-three-dot diff artefact. It was not: the three-dot diff showed the reverted files too, because the bogus parent made main an ancestor. VERIFY a scope complaint against `git merge-base` + `git ls-tree` before rebutting it — "the bot is confused about diff bases" is a plausible-sounding rebuttal that would have merged a silent revert of a shipped money-correctness fix.
- First seen in: #2148 (2026-07-29), caught by the Claude review bot on PR #2166; the reverted work was #2133 (PR #2165, merged ~20 minutes earlier in the same session).
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `.claude/skills/engineering/pre-push-checklist.md` (branch-scope diff check).

## A normalisation applied where cell text feeds SELECTION is a different change from the same normalisation applied where a value is READ

- Symptom: #2164 needed zero-width spacers (U+200B et al) scrubbed so `'<ZWSP> 17,464 (2)'` would parse as a share count — the Item 402(c) path had scrubbed them since #1945, the Item 403 path never learned. Putting the scrub in `_strip_inline_html`, the shared cell-text helper, looked like the obvious chokepoint and passed Codex, 114 unit tests and the 5-filing panel. The full-population A/B then showed **35 genuine holders lost across ~12 accessions** — Vanguard, BlackRock, Dimensional, Harris and Victory on `0001558370-25-003243` alone. `_strip_inline_html` feeds header text into `_score_table_headers`, `_looks_like_label_row` and the two-row-header promotion, so scrubbing there changes WHICH TABLE WINS: that filing's Item 403(b) table has a header row that is entirely U+200B, scores 0 on `main`, and its window falls below the floor — so the loop moves on to the window holding the 403(a) 5%-holder table at 15. Emptying the header promoted the caption row, the table scored 4, the EARLIER window cleared the floor and won first, and the 5%-holder window was never reached. Same round also gained bare address lines (`'malvern, pa 19355'`) as holder names.
- Prevention: before adding a normalisation to a shared text helper, grep every consumer and split them into "reads a value" and "decides which candidate wins". If any consumer is in the second group, the normalisation does not belong there — put it at the points that read meaning (`_parse_share_count`, `_parse_percent`, `_clean_beneficial_holder_name` here) and say so in a comment on the helper you did NOT change, so the next person does not re-apply it. A normalisation that alters a SCORE alters selection, and selection changes are a different blast radius from parsing changes even when the character class is identical. Corollary: a fix can be correct and still be in the wrong place — "the fix works on the failing case" does not bound what else it touches.
- First seen in: #2164 (2026-07-29), A/B round 1. Not caught by Codex ckpt-2, which approved the reverted design.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_def14a.py::_strip_inline_html` (why-NOT comment) + `_parse_share_count` / `_parse_percent` / `_clean_beneficial_holder_name`; `tests/test_sec_def14a_parser.py::TestZeroWidthSpacersInValueCells::test_zero_width_is_stripped_at_the_value_parsers_not_upstream`.

## Widening a regex by adding `\b` anchors NARROWS it — a leading word boundary cannot match inside a compound noun

- Symptom: #2164 made the Item 403 5%-holder role heading order-insensitive so `'Other Shareowners that Beneficially Own More than 5%'` (noun before the threshold, `shareowners` not `holders`) would match. The rewrite replaced `5\s*%.*holders?` with a lookahead pair whose noun was anchored `\b(?:share|stock)?(?:holders?|owners?)\b`. The old pattern had no leading boundary and matched the SUFFIX of a compound noun; the new one cannot. `'5% Equityholders'` therefore stopped matching, `current_role` was never set, and **40 holders lost the `principal` role they had on `main`** — BlackRock, The Vanguard Group, State Street and Alclear Investments across `0001193125-26-170704`, `-25-096950`, `-26-173833`. A change made purely to widen coverage silently removed coverage.
- Prevention: when replacing a regex, enumerate what the OLD one matched that the new one does not, not only the new cases you set out to add — an unanchored `.*noun` accepts compounds (`equityholders`, `unitholders`, `noteholders`) that `\bnoun\b` rejects. Issuers compound these freely. Keep a trailing `\b` where you need to exclude a longer word (`ownership`), and drop the leading one. Table-test the compounds explicitly rather than trusting the intent of the edit.
- First seen in: #2164 (2026-07-29), A/B round 2, found by the role audit (arm 3) — invisible to arms 1 and 2, which key on holder NAME.
- Enforced in: this prevention log; `app/providers/implementations/sec_def14a.py::_ROLE_HEADING_PATTERNS` (no-leading-boundary comment); `tests/test_sec_def14a_parser.py::TestFivePercentHeadingIsOrderInsensitive::test_compound_holder_nouns_still_match` + `test_ownership_is_not_a_holder_noun`.

## The Nth-consecutive-patch trigger is only useful if you act on it — the spec was literally counting the patches while a fifth was written

- First seen in: #2160 (DEF 14A Item 403 table selection). CLAUDE.md's
  "Standard-filing reuse check" mandates checking edgartools + structured
  sources before patching a parser for a standard filing, and names the trigger:
  **"A recurring per-case patch is the trigger to run this check."** The spec
  being edited said, in its own prose, that the selector "has now needed four
  consecutive per-case patches (#2140 D7, #2158 element 4, #2157, #2160)". A
  fifth was designed, measured across the full corpus, and taken to a pushed
  branch without the check ever being run.
- When it was finally run, it took ~10 minutes and changed the design input:
  `edgar.proxy.html_extractor.extract_beneficial_ownership` exists, is NOT a
  drop-in (returns `None` on 4 of our 8 genuine failures and under-extracts a
  fifth: 2 rows where `main` found 16), but it solves the two largest losses
  outright WITH percentages — and its `_build_column_map` carries the technique
  our gate lacks: **when headers classify too few columns, classify the first
  two DATA ROWS instead.** Our D4 is header-only, which is exactly why the
  44-accession "degraded header" bucket fails. That technique had been written
  off in my own notes as a novel unmeasured idea.
- Prevention: treat the trigger as MECHANICAL, not a judgement call. If a spec,
  commit message or issue names a count of prior per-case patches to the same
  function, the reuse check is DUE before the next one — run it and record the
  outcome in the spec's Source-rule section, including a negative result
  ("edgartools tested on accessions X/Y/Z, returns None on N of them"). A
  reuse check that is skipped costs a whole design round; #2160 burned one.
- Also: "I found no prior art" is not a licence to keep patching. Item 403 is
  unstructured BY REGULATION (17 CFR 240.14a-101 mandates content, not form),
  so no dedicated parser exists — but the same issuer data IS machine-readable
  elsewhere (Schedule 13D/G structured XML since 2024-12-18, Forms 3/4/5), and
  this repo already ingests both. Ask what the STRUCTURED arbiter is before
  writing another HTML heuristic.
- Enforced in: this log; `.claude/skills/data-sources/edgartools.md` (DEF 14A
  row now names `extract_beneficial_ownership` and the data-row fallback).

### `<details>` does not defer render cost — React commits its children regardless
- First seen in: #2178
- Symptom: The reports appendix wrapped `JSON.stringify(snap, null, 2)` in a
  `<details>` element, reading as "only built when opened". `<details>`
  collapses **visually**; React still evaluates the expression and commits the
  `<pre>` on every render. A monthly snapshot was ~4.4 MB, so every render
  materialised ~9 MB of text plus a giant text node the operator never asked
  for. Confirmed live: `<pre>` count was 0 after the state fix and only became
  1 on click.
- Prevention: Gate expensive children on **state**, not on a disclosure
  element. `<details>`/`hidden`/`display:none`/CSS collapse are all
  presentation-only — they change paint, never the React commit. Before
  wrapping anything in a disclosure widget, ask "does the JSX inside cost
  anything to *build*?" If yes (JSON.stringify, a large `.map`, a chart),
  render `null` until opened. Grep prompt for review: any `<details>` whose
  body contains `JSON.stringify`, `.map(`, or a component that takes a large
  array prop.
- Enforced in: this log; `frontend/src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx::RawJsonAppendix`
  and its test "does not build the raw-JSON appendix until the operator opens it".

---

### A snapshot-shape change breaks a contract chain the fast tier cannot see
- First seen in: #2178
- Symptom: Adding one top-level key (`score_changes_total`) to the report
  builders silently broke the deliberate chain
  **builder == fixture == FE key list == interface**. The parity test
  (`tests/test_reporting_v2_db.py::test_v2_fixture_is_backend_emitted`) is
  `db`-marked, so `pytest -m "not db"` — the push gate — never ran it. Codex
  ckpt-2 caught it; the local gates were all green and would have shipped it.
- Prevention: Any change to a top-level key emitted by `generate_weekly_report`
  / `generate_monthly_report` must, in the SAME diff: (1) regenerate the
  fixtures with `REPORT_FIXTURE_WRITE=1 uv run pytest
  tests/test_reporting_v2_db.py -k fixture_is_backend_emitted`, (2) update
  `WEEKLY_KEYS`/`MONTHLY_KEYS` in `frontend/src/api/reportSnapshot.test.ts`,
  (3) update the interface in `reportSnapshot.ts`. Because the guard is
  `db`-marked, run that file explicitly — do not rely on the push gate.
- Secondary finding: the regeneration surfaced an UNRELATED stale value the
  parity test could never catch — the fixture's `sector` read `"Technology"`
  where the builder emits `"Information Technology"` (#1634/#1851/#1951). The
  parity test compares **keys only**, so fixture *values* drift silently and
  indefinitely. When regenerating any golden fixture, diff the values too and
  account for every change; a value diff is either a real regression or a
  staleness that has been lying to tests since it appeared.
- Enforced in: this log.

### Two supervisors for one singleton: the loser parks forever and its feature dies silently
- First seen in: #2187 (2026-08-02). A launchd agent (`com.ebull.jobs-daemon`,
  `KeepAlive` + `ThrottleInterval=60`) and the VS Code `stack: jobs` task both
  launched the jobs daemon. The task `kill -9`-ed **both** process patterns
  (`app\.jobs(\.dev_reload)?`) and started `app.jobs.dev_reload`; launchd
  respawned its own copy inside the throttle window and won the PG
  advisory-lock race. `dev_reload`'s child FATAL-exited, and its supervisor
  only respawns on a `*.py` change — never on a tick — so it parked at ~19 MB
  indefinitely. Net effect: **#2144's auto-reload-on-merge was dead for weeks
  while both processes looked healthy**, and every jobs/parser merge silently
  went back to needing the manual restart #2144 existed to remove.
- Symptom: a process singleton guarded by an advisory lock / PID file has more
  than one launcher (init system + IDE task + `nohup` runbook line). Because
  the lock does its job, there is no error and no duplicate work — the visible
  state is one healthy daemon. What is lost is whatever the *losing*
  supervisor provided (a file watcher, a log tail, a restart policy), and that
  loss is invisible precisely because the lock made the failure graceful.
  `pgrep`-ing for the daemon confirms "it's running" and tells you nothing.
- Prevention: a supervised singleton gets **exactly one launcher**, named in
  the launcher's own comment, and every other entry point delegates to it
  rather than starting its own copy (here: `.vscode/tasks.json` runs
  `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$UID/com.ebull.jobs-daemon` and tails the
  agent's log). When adding a supervisor wrapper around an existing daemon,
  check for other launchers **before** shipping: `launchctl list | grep <name>`,
  `grep -rn "<module>" .vscode/ scripts/ docs/`. Judge a supervisor by the
  behaviour it provides, not by the child being alive — the #2144 tell was
  the supervisor's RSS parked at ~19 MB, never that the daemon was down.
- Enforced in: this log; `scripts/autonomy/com.ebull.jobs-daemon.plist`
  (the "THERE MUST BE EXACTLY ONE LAUNCHER" block);
  `scripts/autonomy/README.md` ("Exactly one launcher"); `.vscode/tasks.json`
  (`stack: jobs` delegates to `launchctl kickstart`).

### A resident local-LLM model is a standing memory cost, not a per-call one
- First seen in: #2187 (2026-08-02). The OOM was diagnosed twice from the
  wrong premise — first "something set a 12h `keep_alive`", then "a duplicate
  daemon double-polls". Both were falsified by measurement: `api/ps` showed
  `expires_at = now + 5m` **sliding** (the default timer, refreshed by every
  request), and the launchd log showed 4-5 theses/hour with zero variance
  across 24 hours. The real mechanism needed no bug at all — `thesis_refresh`
  is hourly with ≤5 generations at ≈260s each, so the model is resident ~27 of
  every 60 minutes, 24/7, **by design**. On Apple silicon that is WIRED
  unified memory, which cannot be paged out.
- Symptom: an inference-server model appears to be "stuck" in memory. Every
  keep-alive/TTL lever looks like the fix and none of them help, because a
  polling caller refreshes the rolling timer faster than it can expire. The
  TTL only ever governs the tail after the LAST call, so tuning it is close to
  a no-op whenever the batch is longer than the TTL.
- Prevention: before tuning any TTL, measure the caller's duty cycle
  (`grep`-count the job's completions per hour in its log) and compare it to
  the TTL. If duty-cycle ≫ TTL, the lever is an **explicit release after the
  batch**, not a shorter timer. Release once per batch, never per call — the
  model must stay warm across back-to-back generations. Put the release in
  `finally` (a batch that dies mid-way is exactly when the weights would
  otherwise sit until the next fire) and behind the "did we load anything?"
  early return, so a no-op run cannot evict another consumer's model on a
  shared box. Note that `keep_alive` is **not** an OpenAI field: sending it in
  a `/v1/chat/completions` body looks like a fix and does nothing — Ollama's
  unload route is native (`POST /api/generate {"keep_alive": 0}`) and lives at
  the server ROOT, not under `/v1`.
- Enforced in: this log;
  `app/services/llm_client.py::OpenAICompatProvider.release_model` +
  `release_local_models`; `app/workers/scheduler.py::thesis_refresh` (the
  `finally` release); `tests/test_thesis_refresh_lock.py`
  (`test_batch_releases_local_model_when_a_generation_raises`,
  `test_empty_batch_does_not_release`).

---

### A range CHECK belongs on the derived layer — the source-faithful landing table legitimately holds sentinels
- First seen in: #2192 (2026-08-02). `financial_periods.fiscal_year` had no range CHECK while its sibling `fiscal_quarter` has had `BETWEEN 1 AND 4` since sql/032 (the #1955 class in DDL). The obvious fix — constrain every `fiscal_year` column — would have been wrong: `financial_facts_raw` holds **3,188 rows with `fiscal_year = 0` across 23 instruments**, and that zero is SEC's own. companyfacts emits `{"fy": 0, "fp": ""}` for facts from filings with no fiscal-period focus (1,825 such entries for OLN alone). A CHECK there would reject legitimate source data and convert a filer's tagging error into an ingest outage.
- Symptom: a column appears in several tables across an ingest chain and a validation rule is applied uniformly to all of them. The layers have different jobs — the landing table records **what the source said** (evidence, auditability), the derived table carries **a computed label** that downstream metrics key on — so a rule that is correct for one is destructive for the other. The tell is a "violation" count that is large in the landing table and tiny in the derived one.
- Prevention: before adding a CHECK/NOT NULL to a column that exists at more than one layer, classify each table as source-faithful or derived, and constrain only the derived ones. Then check the **producer**: if the normalizer can still emit an out-of-range value, the constraint turns today's silent skew into tomorrow's hard ingest failure, so the producer guard and the constraint must land in the SAME PR. Order inside the migration is repair → constrain, never the reverse. Verify the sentinel question at the source (`data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/...`), never by inference — here the source itself publishes both the sentinel (`fy: 0`) and the corrupt values (PRTH `fy=43830`, an Excel serial; WTBA `fy=2107`, a transposition), so "our parser must be coercing a date" was the wrong premise and would have sent the fix to the wrong file.
- Secondary finding: a `CHECK` referencing `CURRENT_DATE` is **accepted** by Postgres — it is not rejected for non-immutability, contrary to the natural assumption. It is still the wrong choice, because a row valid at insert silently becomes invalid later, breaking `pg_dump`/restore and making `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` drift with the clock. Use a fixed bound and say why.
- Enforced in: this log; `sql/243_fiscal_year_range_check.sql` (constrains `financial_periods`, `financial_periods_raw`, `def14a_exec_compensation`; documents why `financial_facts_raw` is excluded); `app/services/fundamentals/__init__.py::_is_plausible_fiscal_year` + the anchor-build and quarterly-path guards; `tests/test_financial_normalization.py::TestFiscalYearRangeGuard2192`.
